- **C — augment:** Meaning to make larger or greater in size or extent (synonym: expand; neutral tone), 'augment' indicates addition rather than reduction.
- **Exam Extra:** Top SSC synonyms for 'abate' are 'subside', 'wane', 'diminish', and 'dwindle'; its antonym is 'intensify' or 'aggravate'.

### Q262 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "abhor".

A. admire
B. cherish
C. detest
D. adore

**Answer:** C. detest
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — detest (Correct):** 'Abhor' (verb, strong negative tone) means to regard with intense disgust and hatred, which matches 'detest'.
- **A — admire:** Meaning to regard with deep respect and warm approval (synonym: esteem; positive tone), 'admire' is an antonym of 'abhor'.
- **B — cherish:** Meaning to love and care for deeply (synonym: treasure; positive tone), 'cherish' expresses affection rather than disgust.
- **D — adore:** Meaning to love or respect deeply, often with reverence (synonym: idolize; positive tone), 'adore' contrasts sharply with hatred.
- **Exam Extra:** 'Abhor' (verb) -> 'abhorrence' (noun) -> 'abhorrent' (adjective); close SSC synonyms include 'loathe' and 'despise'.

### Q263 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "abundant".

A. plentiful
B. scarce
C. sparse
D. deficient

**Answer:** A. plentiful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — plentiful (Correct):** 'Abundant' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means existing or available in large quantities, which matches 'plentiful'.
- **B — scarce:** Meaning insufficient for the demand or rare (synonym: scanty; negative tone), 'scarce' is an antonym of 'abundant'.
- **C — sparse:** Meaning small in quantity or inadequate (synonym: meagre; negative tone), 'sparse' denotes thin or scattered presence.
- **D — deficient:** Meaning lacking in quality or quantity (synonym: inadequate; negative tone), 'deficient' indicates a shortage rather than plenty.
- **Exam Extra:** Latin 'ab-' (away) + 'unda' (wave) literally means overflowing like a wave; SSC synonyms include 'copious' and 'profuse'.

### Q264 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "accurate".

A. erroneous
B. precise
C. arbitrary
D. vague

**Answer:** B. precise
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — precise (Correct):** 'Accurate' (adjective, positive tone) means correct in all details and free from error, which matches 'precise'.
- **A — erroneous:** Meaning containing mistakes or wrong information (synonym: flawed; negative tone), 'erroneous' is an antonym of 'accurate'.
- **C — arbitrary:** Meaning based on random choice or personal whim (synonym: erratic; negative tone), 'arbitrary' lacks exactness.
- **D — vague:** Meaning not clearly expressed or indefinite (synonym: imprecise; negative tone), 'vague' contrasts with exact accuracy.
- **Exam Extra:** In scientific and SSC usage, 'accurate' (correct/true) and 'precise' (exact/detailed) frequently appear as standard synonyms.

### Q265 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "adamant".

A. compliant
B. pliable
C. unyielding
D. vacillating

**Answer:** C. unyielding
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — unyielding (Correct):** 'Adamant' (adjective, negative/emphatic tone) means refusing to be persuaded or to change one's mind, which matches 'unyielding'.
- **A — compliant:** Meaning easily persuaded to give in or obey (synonym: submissive; neutral/negative tone), 'compliant' is an antonym of 'adamant'.
- **B — pliable:** Meaning able to bend or change easily (synonym: adaptable; positive tone), 'pliable' denotes readiness to yield.
- **D — vacillating:** Meaning hesitating or unable to make a firm decision (synonym: indecisive; negative tone), 'vacillating' indicates wavering rather than firmness.
- **Exam Extra:** Originally a noun meaning an unbreakable stone or diamond; SSC synonyms include 'inflexible', 'obdurate', and 'resolute'.

### Q266 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "adversity".

A. prosperity
B. luxury
C. windfall
D. hardship

**Answer:** D. hardship
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — hardship (Correct):** 'Adversity' (noun, negative tone) means a difficult or unpleasant situation, misfortune, or state of suffering, which matches 'hardship'.
- **A — prosperity:** Meaning a state of flourishing success and wealth (synonym: affluence; positive tone), 'prosperity' is an antonym of 'adversity'.
- **B — luxury:** Meaning great comfort and extravagant living (synonym: opulence; positive/neutral tone), 'luxury' denotes ease rather than difficulty.
- **C — windfall:** Meaning good fortune or a fortunate stroke of luck (synonym: blessing; positive tone), 'windfall' represents unexpected gain.
- **Exam Extra:** Pair to remember: 'adversity' (misfortune) vs 'prosperity' (success/wealth), both frequent in SSC antonym and cloze-test passages.

### Q267 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "amiable".

A. hostile
B. friendly
C. surly
D. aloof

**Answer:** B. friendly
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — friendly (Correct):** 'Amiable' (adjective, positive tone) means having or displaying a pleasant, good-natured manner, which matches 'friendly'.
- **A — hostile:** Meaning showing or feeling opposition or dislike (synonym: antagonistic; negative tone), 'hostile' is an antonym of 'amiable'.
- **C — surly:** Meaning bad-tempered, irritable, and unfriendly (synonym: grumpy; negative tone), 'surly' conveys rudeness rather than warmth.
- **D — aloof:** Meaning cold, distant, and uninvolved (synonym: detached; negative/neutral tone), 'aloof' lacks friendliness.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'amicus' (friend); note the subtle difference between 'amiable' (friendly in manner) and 'amicable' (harmonious between parties, e.g., an amicable settlement).

### Q268 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "benevolent".

A. kind
B. malevolent
C. ruthless
D. mean

**Answer:** A. kind
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — kind (Correct):** 'Benevolent' (adjective, positive tone) means well-meaning, charitable, and kindly disposed, which matches 'kind'.
- **B — malevolent:** Meaning having or showing a wish to do evil to others (synonym: spiteful; negative tone), 'malevolent' is the direct antonym of 'benevolent'.
- **C — ruthless:** Meaning cruel, harsh, and completely without pity (synonym: brutal; negative tone), 'ruthless' shows no kindness.
- **D — mean:** Meaning unwilling to spend or give (synonym: miserly; negative tone), 'mean' implies selfishness rather than generosity.
- **Exam Extra:** Root breakdown: 'bene-' (good/well) + 'volens' (wishing). Contrast with 'malevolent' ('male-' = evil/bad).

### Q269 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "candid".

A. frank
B. evasive
C. devious
D. guarded

**Answer:** A. frank
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — frank (Correct):** 'Candid' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means truthful, straightforward, and outspoken, which matches 'frank'.
- **B — evasive:** Meaning tending to avoid giving direct answers (synonym: elusive; negative tone), 'evasive' is an antonym of 'candid'.
- **C — devious:** Meaning acting in a secret or dishonest way (synonym: sly; negative tone), 'devious' implies deceit rather than openness.
- **D — guarded:** Meaning expressing ideas indirectly or carefully (synonym: discreet; neutral/positive tone), 'guarded' withholds full honesty.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'candidus' (white/pure); in SSC exams, 'candid' frequently pairs with 'forthright' and 'outspoken' as synonyms.

### Q270 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "cease".

A. commence
B. stop
C. persevere
D. prolong

**Answer:** B. stop
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — stop (Correct):** 'Cease' (verb, neutral tone) means to bring or come to an end, which matches 'stop'.
- **A — commence:** Meaning to start or begin something (synonym: initiate; neutral tone), 'commence' is an antonym of 'cease'.
- **C — persevere:** Meaning to continue in an action despite difficulty (synonym: persist; positive/neutral tone), 'persevere' implies keeping going rather than stopping.
- **D — prolong:** Meaning to make something last longer (synonym: extend; neutral tone), 'prolong' delays the end rather than bringing it.
- **Exam Extra:** Notice the noun form 'cessation' (stopping) and the adjective 'ceaseless' (never stopping), both tested in SSC vocabulary sets.

### Q271 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "coherent".

A. disjointed
B. incoherent
C. logical
D. rambling

**Answer:** C. logical
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — logical (Correct):** 'Coherent' (adjective, positive tone) means unified, clear, and logically consistent, which matches 'logical'.
- **A — disjointed:** Meaning lacking order, clarity, or logical connection (synonym: muddled; negative tone), 'disjointed' is an antonym of 'coherent'.
- **B — incoherent:** Meaning spoken or written in an unclear, confused way (synonym: unintelligible; negative tone), 'incoherent' expresses the opposite sense.
- **D — rambling:** Meaning wandering from topic to topic without aim (synonym: erratic; negative tone), 'rambling' lacks structure and clarity.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'cohaerere' (to stick together); compare 'coherent' (logically connected) with 'inherent' (existing naturally within).

### Q272 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "concise".

A. verbose
B. lengthy
C. prolix
D. brief

**Answer:** D. brief
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — brief (Correct):** 'Concise' (adjective, positive tone) means giving a lot of information clearly and in a few words, which matches 'brief'.
- **A — verbose:** Meaning using more words than are needed (synonym: wordy; negative tone), 'verbose' is an antonym of 'concise'.
- **B — lengthy:** Meaning tediously long and drawn out (synonym: protracted; negative tone), 'lengthy' implies excessive duration or word count.
- **C — prolix:** Meaning repetitively wordy and indirect (synonym: circuitous; negative tone), 'prolix' is the opposite of brevity.
- **Exam Extra:** Strong SSC synonym cluster for 'concise': 'brief', 'succinct', 'terse', 'laconic', and 'pithy'.

### Q273 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "elated".

A. dejected
B. joyful
C. melancholy
D. despondent

**Answer:** B. joyful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — joyful (Correct):** 'Elated' (adjective, positive tone) means ecstatically happy and in high spirits, which matches 'joyful'.
- **A — dejected:** Meaning sad and depressed or dispirited (synonym: downcast; negative tone), 'dejected' is an antonym of 'elated'.
- **C — melancholy:** Meaning feeling or showing deep sorrow (synonym: gloomy; negative tone), 'melancholy' indicates lasting sadness.
- **D — despondent:** Meaning feeling or expressing hopelessness (synonym: demoralized; negative tone), 'despondent' reflects a loss of spirit.
- **Exam Extra:** Frequent SSC synonyms for 'elated' are 'jubilant', 'ecstatic', and 'euphoric', while 'dejected' and 'despondent' are standard antonyms.

### Q274 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "exalt".

A. rebuke
B. belittle
C. debase
D. praise

**Answer:** D. praise
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — praise (Correct):** 'Exalt' (verb, positive tone) means to think, speak, or write very highly of someone or something, which matches 'praise'.
- **A — rebuke:** Meaning to criticize or reprimand severely (synonym: censure; negative tone), 'rebuke' is an antonym of 'exalt'.
- **B — belittle:** Meaning to make someone or something seem unimportant (synonym: disparage; negative tone), 'belittle' lowers worth rather than elevating it.
- **C — debase:** Meaning to lower in character, quality, or esteem (synonym: degrade; negative tone), 'debase' is the opposite of lifting up.
- **Exam Extra:** Do not confuse 'exalt' (praise/elevate in rank or regard) with 'exult' (feel or show triumphant joy).

### Q275 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "impromptu".

A. extempore
B. rehearsed
C. deliberate
D. scripted

**Answer:** A. extempore
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — extempore (Correct):** 'Impromptu' (adjective, neutral tone) means done without being planned, organized, or rehearsed in advance, which matches 'extempore'.
- **B — rehearsed:** Meaning practised or prepared in advance (synonym: scripted; neutral tone), 'rehearsed' is an antonym of 'impromptu'.
- **C — deliberate:** Meaning carefully planned or thought out beforehand (synonym: intentional; neutral tone), 'deliberate' implies prior calculation.
- **D — scripted:** Meaning prepared and written out carefully before delivery (synonym: drafted; neutral tone), 'scripted' contrasts with spontaneous speech.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC PYQs, 'impromptu', 'extempore', and 'improvised' form a standard synonym triad, opposed to 'rehearsed' or 'premeditated'.

### Q276 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "inevitable".

A. avoidable
B. contingent
C. unavoidable
D. uncertain

**Answer:** C. unavoidable
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — unavoidable (Correct):** 'Inevitable' (adjective, neutral tone) means certain to happen and impossible to prevent, which matches 'unavoidable'.
- **A — avoidable:** Meaning able to be prevented or kept from happening (synonym: preventable; neutral tone), 'avoidable' is the direct antonym of 'inevitable'.
- **B — contingent:** Meaning dependent on chance or uncertain circumstances (synonym: conditional; neutral tone), 'contingent' implies uncertainty rather than certainty.
- **D — uncertain:** Meaning not definite and liable to change (synonym: doubtful; negative/neutral tone), 'uncertain' lacks the certainty of 'inevitable'.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'evitare' (to avoid) + 'in-' (not); another high-frequency SSC synonym is 'inescapable'.

### Q277 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "skeptic".

A. believer
B. zealot
C. dupe
D. doubter

**Answer:** D. doubter
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — doubter (Correct):** 'Skeptic' (noun, neutral tone) means a person inclined to question or doubt accepted opinions and claims, which matches 'doubter'.
- **A — believer:** Meaning a person who accepts a claim or faith as true (synonym: adherent; positive/neutral tone), 'believer' is an antonym of 'skeptic'.
- **B — zealot:** Meaning a person who is fanatical and uncompromising in pursuit of a cause (synonym: fanatic; negative tone), 'zealot' implies blind conviction rather than doubt.
- **C — dupe:** Meaning a person who believes everything easily without proof (synonym: innocent; negative tone), 'dupe' denotes someone easily fooled.
- **Exam Extra:** In American spelling it is 'skeptic' and in British spelling 'sceptic' — SSC accepts both and often pairs it with 'cynic' or 'agnostic' depending on context.

### Q278 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "panacea".

A. cure-all
B. ailment
C. venom
D. symptom

**Answer:** A. cure-all
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — cure-all (Correct):** 'Panacea' (noun, positive tone) means a solution or remedy for all difficulties or diseases, which matches 'cure-all'.
- **B — ailment:** Meaning an illness, typically a minor one (synonym: malady; negative tone), 'ailment' is a sickness rather than a cure.
- **C — venom:** Meaning a substance that causes illness or death when absorbed (synonym: toxin; negative tone), 'venom' harms rather than heals.
- **D — symptom:** Meaning a physical or mental feature indicating disease (synonym: sign; neutral/negative tone), 'symptom' is an indicator of illness, not its remedy.
- **Exam Extra:** From Greek 'pan-' (all) + 'akos' (remedy); 'pan-' also appears in 'pandemic' (affecting all people) and 'pandemonium'.

### Q279 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "philanthropist".

A. misanthrope
B. miser
C. altruist
D. egoist

**Answer:** C. altruist
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — altruist (Correct):** 'Philanthropist' (noun, positive tone) means a person who seeks to promote the welfare of others, especially through generous donations, which matches 'altruist'.
- **A — misanthrope:** Meaning a person who dislikes humankind and avoids human society (synonym: cynic; negative tone), 'misanthrope' is the direct antonym of 'philanthropist'.
- **B — miser:** Meaning a person who hoards wealth and spends as little as possible (synonym: skinflint; negative tone), 'miser' contrasts with charitable giving.
- **D — egoist:** Meaning a self-centred person concerned only with their own interests (synonym: narcissist; negative tone), 'egoist' is the opposite of an altruist.
- **Exam Extra:** Root breakdown: 'phil-' (love) + 'anthropos' (mankind); contrast with 'misanthrope' ('mis-' = hate + 'anthropos' = mankind).

### Q280 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "ubiquitous".

A. rare
B. omnipresent
C. localized
D. infrequent

**Answer:** B. omnipresent
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — omnipresent (Correct):** 'Ubiquitous' (adjective, neutral tone) means present, appearing, or found everywhere, which matches 'omnipresent'.
- **A — rare:** Meaning occurring rarely or found only in a few places (synonym: scarce; neutral tone), 'rare' is an antonym of 'ubiquitous'.
- **C — localized:** Meaning restricted to a particular area or group (synonym: regional; neutral tone), 'localized' denotes limited reach rather than universal presence.
- **D — infrequent:** Meaning seldom seen or happening very few times (synonym: occasional; neutral tone), 'infrequent' contrasts with everywhere-at-once presence.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'ubique' (everywhere); both 'ubiquitous' and 'omnipresent' ('omni-' = all) are among the top 20 SSC CGL Tier-2 words.

### Q281 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "taciturn".

A. garrulous
B. eloquent
C. reticent
D. loquacious

**Answer:** C. reticent
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — reticent (Correct):** 'Taciturn' (adjective, neutral/negative tone) means habitually reserved or uncommunicative in speech, which matches 'reticent'.
- **A — garrulous:** Meaning excessively talkative, especially on trivial matters (synonym: chatty; negative tone), 'garrulous' is an antonym of 'taciturn'.
- **B — eloquent:** Meaning fluent and persuasive in speaking or writing (synonym: articulate; positive tone), 'eloquent' implies expressive speech rather than silence.
- **D — loquacious:** Meaning tending to talk a great deal (synonym: talkative; neutral/negative tone), 'loquacious' is the opposite of reserved speech.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'tacitus' (silent), which also gives 'tacit' (understood without being stated); antonym is 'garrulous' or 'loquacious'.

### Q282 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "ostentatious".

A. modest
B. showy
C. unobtrusive
D. austere

**Answer:** B. showy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — showy (Correct):** 'Ostentatious' (adjective, negative tone) means characterized by vulgar or pretentious display designed to impress, which matches 'showy'.
- **A — modest:** Meaning unassuming in the estimation of one's abilities or wealth (synonym: humble; positive tone), 'modest' is an antonym of 'ostentatious'.
- **C — unobtrusive:** Meaning not attracting attention or seeking publicity (synonym: discreet; positive tone), 'unobtrusive' avoids flashy display.
- **D — austere:** Meaning plain, simple, and strict without luxury (synonym: ascetic; neutral tone), 'austere' is the opposite of flashy extravagance.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC exams, 'ostentatious' is routinely paired with 'flamboyant', 'pretentious', and 'gaudy', and contrasted with 'modest'.

### Q283 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "parsimonious".

A. generous
B. munificent
C. prodigal
D. stingy

**Answer:** D. stingy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — stingy (Correct):** 'Parsimonious' (adjective, negative tone) means extremely unwilling to spend money or use resources, which matches 'stingy'.
- **A — generous:** Meaning showing a readiness to give more than is expected (synonym: charitable; positive tone), 'generous' is an antonym of 'parsimonious'.
- **B — munificent:** Meaning extremely generous and free-giving (synonym: liberal; positive tone), 'munificent' denotes lavish giving.
- **C — prodigal:** Meaning spending money or resources freely and recklessly (synonym: wasteful; negative tone), 'prodigal' is the opposite of stinginess.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember the difference: 'frugal' is wise saving (positive), while 'parsimonious' and 'niggardly' mean excessively stingy (negative).

### Q284 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "magnanimous".

A. generous
B. vindictive
C. petty
D. acrimonious

**Answer:** A. generous
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — generous (Correct):** 'Magnanimous' (adjective, positive tone) means generous or forgiving, especially towards a rival or less powerful person, which matches 'generous'.
- **B — vindictive:** Meaning feeling or showing resentment at another's success (synonym: envious; negative tone), 'vindictive' seeks revenge rather than forgiveness.
- **C — petty:** Meaning small-minded and overly concerned with trivial matters (synonym: spiteful; negative tone), 'petty' is an antonym of 'magnanimous'.
- **D — acrimonious:** Meaning bitter and angry in tone or behaviour (synonym: resentful; negative tone), 'acrimonious' lacks noble generosity.
- **Exam Extra:** Root breakdown: 'magn-' (great) + 'animus' (soul/spirit) = great-souled; contrast with 'pusillanimous' (small-souled/cowardly).

### Q285 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "mitigate".

A. alleviate
B. exacerbate
C. incite
D. magnify

**Answer:** A. alleviate
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — alleviate (Correct):** 'Mitigate' (verb, positive/neutral tone) means to make something less severe, harmful, or painful, which matches 'alleviate'.
- **B — exacerbate:** Meaning to make a problem, bad situation, or negative feeling worse (synonym: intensify; negative tone), 'exacerbate' is an antonym of 'mitigate'.
- **C — incite:** Meaning to provoke or stir up violent feelings (synonym: instigate; negative tone), 'incite' worsens conflict instead of easing it.
- **D — magnify:** Meaning to make something seem bigger or more serious (synonym: exaggerate; negative tone), 'magnify' increases rather than reduces severity.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC synonyms for 'mitigate' include 'allay', 'assuage', 'appease', and 'pacify'; antonyms include 'aggravate' and 'exacerbate'.

### Q286 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "pragmatic".

A. idealistic
B. quixotic
C. practical
D. theoretical

**Answer:** C. practical
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — practical (Correct):** 'Pragmatic' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means dealing with things sensibly and realistically rather than theoretically, which matches 'practical'.
- **A — idealistic:** Meaning unrealistically aiming for perfection (synonym: utopian; neutral/positive tone), 'idealistic' prioritizes ideals over practical reality.
- **B — quixotic:** Meaning exceedingly idealistic, unrealistic, and impractical (synonym: visionary; neutral/negative tone), 'quixotic' is an antonym of 'pragmatic'.
- **D — theoretical:** Meaning concerned with abstract principles rather than real application (synonym: hypothetical; neutral tone), 'theoretical' lacks practical focus.
- **Exam Extra:** From Greek 'pragma' (deed/action); in SSC exams, 'pragmatic' is often contrasted with 'idealistic' or 'quixotic'.

### Q287 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "proliferate".

A. dwindle
B. multiply
C. wane
D. stagnate

**Answer:** B. multiply
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — multiply (Correct):** 'Proliferate' (verb, neutral tone) means to increase rapidly in number or spread quickly, which matches 'multiply'.
- **A — dwindle:** Meaning to become gradually smaller or weaker (synonym: shrink; negative/neutral tone), 'dwindle' is an antonym of 'proliferate'.
- **C — wane:** Meaning to decrease in vigour, power, or extent (synonym: decline; neutral tone), 'wane' suggests reduction rather than rapid growth.
- **D — stagnate:** Meaning to stop flowing or developing (synonym: stall; negative tone), 'stagnate' indicates lack of growth.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'proles' (offspring) + 'ferre' (to bear); its noun form 'proliferation' frequently appears in SSC comprehension passages.

### Q288 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "sagacious".

A. fatuous
B. naive
C. obtuse
D. discerning

**Answer:** D. discerning
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — discerning (Correct):** 'Sagacious' (adjective, positive tone) means having or showing keen mental discernment and good judgement, which matches 'discerning'.
- **A — fatuous:** Meaning lacking good sense or judgement (synonym: foolish; negative tone), 'fatuous' is an antonym of 'sagacious'.
- **B — naive:** Meaning showing a lack of experience, wisdom, or judgement (synonym: gullible; negative/neutral tone), 'naive' implies innocence rather than shrewd insight.
- **C — obtuse:** Meaning dull, slow-witted, or annoyingly insensitive (synonym: dense; negative tone), 'obtuse' contrasts with sharp perception.
- **Exam Extra:** Related to the noun 'sage' (a wise person); its close SSC synonyms are 'astute', 'perspicacious', and 'judicious'.

### Q289 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "superfluous".

A. essential
B. redundant
C. requisite
D. fundamental

**Answer:** B. redundant
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — redundant (Correct):** 'Superfluous' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means unnecessary, especially through being more than enough, which matches 'redundant'.
- **A — essential:** Meaning absolutely necessary or extremely important (synonym: vital; positive/neutral tone), 'essential' is an antonym of 'superfluous'.
- **C — requisite:** Meaning needed to be done, achieved, or present (synonym: obligatory; neutral tone), 'requisite' indicates necessity rather than excess.
- **D — fundamental:** Meaning strictly necessary and basic (synonym: indispensable; positive/neutral tone), 'fundamental' cannot be omitted.
- **Exam Extra:** Literally 'overflowing' (Latin 'super-' = over + 'fluere' = to flow); often tested in SSC sentence-improvement items alongside 'redundant'.

### Q290 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "venerate".

A. revere
B. despise
C. desecrate
D. vilify

**Answer:** A. revere
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — revere (Correct):** 'Venerate' (verb, positive tone) means to regard with great respect and reverence, which matches 'revere'.
- **B — despise:** Meaning to consider to be unworthy of one's respect (synonym: scorn; negative tone), 'despise' is an antonym of 'venerate'.
- **C — desecrate:** Meaning to treat a sacred place or thing with violent disrespect (synonym: defile; negative tone), 'desecrate' denotes irreverence rather than worshipful respect.
- **D — vilify:** Meaning to speak or write about in an abusively disparaging manner (synonym: defame; negative tone), 'vilify' attacks reputation rather than honouring it.
- **Exam Extra:** Wait for the contrast in SSC: 'venerate' (respect deeply) vs 'desecrate' or 'disdain' (treat with contempt or irreverence).

### Q291 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "clandestine".

A. overt
B. manifest
C. conspicuous
D. covert

**Answer:** D. covert
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — covert (Correct):** 'Clandestine' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means kept secret or done secretively, especially because illicit, which matches 'covert'.
- **A — overt:** Meaning done or shown openly and plainly (synonym: open; neutral tone), 'overt' is the direct antonym of 'clandestine'.
- **B — manifest:** Meaning clearly visible or understood (synonym: obvious; neutral tone), 'manifest' means plain to see rather than hidden.
- **C — conspicuous:** Meaning attracting notice or clearly visible (synonym: prominent; neutral tone), 'conspicuous' stands out instead of hiding.
- **Exam Extra:** Top SSC synonym group for 'clandestine': 'covert', 'furtive', 'surreptitious', and 'stealthy'; antonym is 'overt' or 'candid'.

### Q292 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "belligerent".

A. pacific
B. docile
C. aggressive
D. meek

**Answer:** C. aggressive
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — aggressive (Correct):** 'Belligerent' (adjective, negative tone) means hostile, ready to fight, and warlike, which matches 'aggressive'.
- **A — pacific:** Meaning peaceable and inclined to avoid conflict (synonym: conciliatory; positive tone), 'pacific' is an antonym of 'belligerent'.
- **B — docile:** Meaning easily taught, submissive, and gentle (synonym: compliant; neutral/positive tone), 'docile' avoids confrontation.
- **D — meek:** Meaning quietly gentle and submissive (synonym: humble; neutral tone), 'meek' lacks fighting spirit.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'bellum' (war), which also gives 'bellicose' (warlike) and 'rebellion' (war back against authority).

### Q293 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "capricious".

A. steadfast
B. consistent
C. constant
D. whimsical

**Answer:** D. whimsical
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — whimsical (Correct):** 'Capricious' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means given to sudden and unaccountable changes of mood or behaviour, which matches 'whimsical'.
- **A — steadfast:** Meaning firmly fixed and not likely to change (synonym: steady; positive/neutral tone), 'steadfast' is an antonym of 'capricious'.
- **B — consistent:** Meaning acting or done in the same reliable way over time (synonym: uniform; positive tone), 'consistent' contrasts with unpredictable mood shifts.
- **C — constant:** Meaning unchanging over time and unwavering (synonym: resolute; positive tone), 'constant' denotes stability.
- **Exam Extra:** Connected to 'caprice' (a sudden whim); frequent SSC synonyms include 'fickle', 'mercurial', and 'erratic'.

### Q294 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "fastidious".

A. slovenly
B. lenient
C. fussy
D. lax

**Answer:** C. fussy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — fussy (Correct):** 'Fastidious' (adjective, neutral/negative tone) means very attentive to accuracy and detail, or hard to please, which matches 'fussy'.
- **A — slovenly:** Meaning untidy, careless, or negligent in appearance or work (synonym: messy; negative tone), 'slovenly' is an antonym of 'fastidious'.
- **B — lenient:** Meaning relaxed and tolerant in attitude or standards (synonym: easy-going; positive/neutral tone), 'lenient' imposes few demands.
- **D — lax:** Meaning indifferent to rules or standards (synonym: casual; negative tone), 'lax' lacks strictness or precision.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC exams, 'fastidious' often carries the nuance of 'finicky', 'particular', or 'fussy' about cleanliness, taste, or detail.

### Q295 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "indolent".

A. slothful
B. strenuous
C. vigorous
D. active

**Answer:** A. slothful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — slothful (Correct):** 'Indolent' (adjective, negative tone) means wanting to avoid activity or exertion, which matches 'slothful'.
- **B — strenuous:** Meaning working hard and energetically (synonym: diligent; positive tone), 'strenuous' requires great effort rather than avoiding it.
- **C — vigorous:** Meaning full of energy and enthusiasm (synonym: lively; positive tone), 'vigorous' is an antonym of 'indolent'.
- **D — active:** Meaning actively engaged and busy (synonym: industrious; positive tone), 'active' contrasts with habitual laziness.
- **Exam Extra:** Note the root 'dolere' (to feel pain) + 'in-' (not) = feeling no pain/effort; close SSC synonyms are 'lethargic', 'torpid', and 'idle'.

### Q296 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "mendacious".

A. veracious
B. deceitful
C. truthful
D. guileless

**Answer:** B. deceitful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — deceitful (Correct):** 'Mendacious' (adjective, negative tone) means not telling the truth or habitually lying, which matches 'deceitful'.
- **A — veracious:** Meaning speaking or representing the truth (synonym: honest; positive tone), 'veracious' is the direct antonym of 'mendacious'.
- **C — truthful:** Meaning completely honest and open in speech (synonym: sincere; positive tone), 'truthful' excludes deception.
- **D — guileless:** Meaning free from deceit and genuine (synonym: straightforward; positive tone), 'guileless' means innocent and honest.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'mendax' (lying); do not confuse 'mendacious' (lying) with 'mendicant' (a beggar).

### Q297 · Synonyms

Select the most appropriate synonym of "pernicious".

A. beneficial
B. salutary
C. harmful
D. innocuous

**Answer:** C. harmful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — harmful (Correct):** 'Pernicious' (adjective, strong negative tone) means having a harmful effect, especially in a gradual or subtle way, which matches 'harmful'.
- **A — beneficial:** Meaning resulting in good or favourable outcomes (synonym: advantageous; positive tone), 'beneficial' is an antonym of 'pernicious'.
- **B — salutary:** Meaning producing good health or moral well-being (synonym: healthy; positive tone), 'salutary' promotes benefit rather than harm.
- **D — innocuous:** Meaning not harmful or offensive (synonym: harmless; neutral/positive tone), 'innocuous' implies zero damage.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'pernicies' (destruction); top SSC synonyms include 'deleterious', 'baneful', and 'detrimental'.

### Q298 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "incessant".

A. intermittent
B. relentless
C. perpetual
D. continual

**Answer:** A. intermittent
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — intermittent (Correct):** 'Incessant' (adjective, neutral/emphatic tone) means continuing without pause; its direct opposite is 'intermittent' (stopping and starting at intervals).
- **B — relentless:** Meaning continuing without pause or interruption (synonym: unceasing; neutral tone), 'relentless' is a synonym of 'incessant', not an antonym.
- **C — perpetual:** Meaning lasting forever or never-ending (synonym: eternal; neutral tone), 'perpetual' reinforces uninterrupted continuity.
- **D — continual:** Meaning occurring everywhere or without end (synonym: endless; neutral tone), 'continual' is very close in meaning to 'incessant'.
- **Exam Extra:** Word pair to lock in: 'incessant' (continuous, ceaseless) ↔ 'intermittent' (sporadic, periodic).

### Q299 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "scrutinize".

A. inspect
B. probe
C. analyse
D. overlook

**Answer:** D. overlook
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — overlook (Correct):** 'Scrutinize' (verb, neutral tone) means to examine or inspect closely and thoroughly; its opposite is 'overlook' (fail to notice or ignore).
- **A — inspect:** Meaning to look at closely in order to assess (synonym: examine; neutral tone), 'inspect' is a synonym of 'scrutinize'.
- **B — probe:** Meaning to look carefully or investigate deeply (synonym: investigate; neutral tone), 'probe' implies thorough examination.
- **C — analyse:** Meaning to examine methodically and in detail (synonym: evaluate; neutral tone), 'analyse' reinforces careful inspection.
- **Exam Extra:** Notice the noun 'scrutiny' (critical observation); SSC frequently pairs 'scrutinize' with 'inspect/probe' (syn) and 'overlook/neglect' (ant).

### Q300 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "peril".

A. hazard
B. safety
C. jeopardy
D. threat

**Answer:** B. safety
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — safety (Correct):** 'Peril' (noun, negative tone) means serious and immediate danger; its opposite is 'safety' (the condition of being protected from harm).
- **A — hazard:** Meaning exposure to the possibility of harm or loss (synonym: risk; negative tone), 'hazard' is a synonym of 'peril'.
- **C — jeopardy:** Meaning danger of loss, harm, or failure (synonym: threat; negative tone), 'jeopardy' conveys immediate danger.
- **D — threat:** Meaning a person or thing likely to cause damage or harm (synonym: menace; negative tone), 'threat' aligns with danger rather than protection.
- **Exam Extra:** The adjective 'perilous' (dangerous) shares the same root; common SSC synonyms for 'peril' are 'hazard' and 'jeopardy'.

### Q301 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "assiduous".

A. meticulous
B. diligent
C. careless
D. painstaking

**Answer:** C. careless
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — careless (Correct):** 'Assiduous' (adjective, positive tone) means showing great care and perseverance; its opposite is 'careless' (not giving sufficient attention or thought).
- **A — meticulous:** Meaning showing great attention to detail (synonym: thorough; positive tone), 'meticulous' is a synonym of 'assiduous'.
- **B — diligent:** Meaning careful and persistent in work or duties (synonym: hardworking; positive tone), 'diligent' reinforces steady care.
- **D — painstaking:** Meaning dedicated and attentive in carrying out duties (synonym: conscientious; positive tone), 'painstaking' implies extreme care.
- **Exam Extra:** Think of 'assiduous' as 'meticulous/attentive' and its opposite as 'careless/negligent/remiss'.

### Q302 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "intricate".

A. complex
B. simple
C. elaborate
D. convoluted

**Answer:** B. simple
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — simple (Correct):** 'Intricate' (adjective, neutral tone) means very complicated or detailed; its opposite is 'simple' (easily understood or done, presenting no difficulty).
- **A — complex:** Meaning consisting of many interconnected parts (synonym: complicated; neutral tone), 'complex' is a synonym of 'intricate'.
- **C — elaborate:** Meaning involving many carefully arranged parts or details (synonym: ornate; neutral tone), 'elaborate' conveys complexity.
- **D — convoluted:** Meaning extremely complex and difficult to follow (synonym: labyrinthine; negative/neutral tone), 'convoluted' reinforces tangling.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC vocab tests, 'intricate' ↔ 'simple/straightforward' is one of the most repeated antonym pairs.

### Q303 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "adapt".

A. adjust
B. accommodate
C. resist
D. acclimatize

**Answer:** C. resist
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — resist (Correct):** 'Adapt' (verb, neutral/positive tone) means to adjust to new conditions or become modified; in attitude, its opposite is 'resist' (withstand or refuse to accept change).
- **A — adjust:** Meaning to alter or change slightly to achieve a better fit (synonym: modify; neutral tone), 'adjust' is a synonym of 'adapt'.
- **B — accommodate:** Meaning to fit in with the wishes or needs of others (synonym: conform; neutral tone), 'accommodate' implies flexibility rather than opposition.
- **D — acclimatize:** Meaning to become accustomed to a new climate or environment (synonym: habituate; neutral tone), 'acclimatize' means to adapt to surroundings.
- **Exam Extra:** Do not confuse 'adapt' (adjust), 'adopt' (take up/accept), and 'adept' (skilled) — a classic SSC fill-in-the-blank trap.

### Q304 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "tedious".

A. monotonous
B. wearisome
C. dreary
D. enjoyable

**Answer:** D. enjoyable
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — enjoyable (Correct):** 'Tedious' (adjective, negative tone) means too long, slow, or dull and tiresome; its opposite is 'enjoyable' (giving delight or pleasure).
- **A — monotonous:** Meaning dull, tedious, and repetitious (synonym: repetitive; negative tone), 'monotonous' is a synonym of 'tedious'.
- **B — wearisome:** Meaning causing someone to feel bored and weary (synonym: tiresome; negative tone), 'wearisome' reinforces fatigue and dullness.
- **C — dreary:** Meaning depressingly dull and bleak (synonym: drab; negative tone), 'dreary' aligns with monotony rather than pleasure.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC synonyms for 'tedious' include 'monotonous', 'dreary', and 'humdrum'; antonyms include 'stimulating', 'engaging', and 'enjoyable'.

### Q305 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "arduous".

A. easy
B. gruelling
C. strenuous
D. laborious

**Answer:** A. easy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — easy (Correct):** 'Arduous' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means involving strenuous effort and difficult; its opposite is 'easy' (achieved without great effort).
- **B — gruelling:** Meaning requiring great effort and endurance (synonym: exhausting; negative/neutral tone), 'gruelling' is a close synonym of 'arduous'.
- **C — strenuous:** Meaning demanding physical or mental effort (synonym: tiring; neutral tone), 'strenuous' matches hard labour.
- **D — laborious:** Meaning needing much effort or skill to accomplish (synonym: tough; neutral tone), 'laborious' reinforces heavy effort.
- **Exam Extra:** Quick memory anchor: 'arduous' (hard/uphill) ↔ 'easy/effortless/facile'.

### Q306 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "prudent".

A. cautious
B. judicious
C. circumspect
D. reckless

**Answer:** D. reckless
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — reckless (Correct):** 'Prudent' (adjective, positive tone) means acting with care and thought for the future; its opposite is 'reckless' (heedless of danger or consequences).
- **A — cautious:** Meaning careful to avoid potential problems or dangers (synonym: wary; positive/neutral tone), 'cautious' is a synonym of 'prudent'.
- **B — judicious:** Meaning discreet and careful in speech or action (synonym: sensible; positive tone), 'judicious' reflects sound judgement.
- **C — circumspect:** Meaning having or showing foresight and care (synonym: careful; positive tone), 'circumspect' implies weighing consequences carefully.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC frequently tests 'prudent' ↔ 'reckless/imprudent/rash' alongside 'cautious' ↔ 'heedless'.

### Q307 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "abundant".

A. plentiful
B. scarce
C. copious
D. profuse

**Answer:** B. scarce
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — scarce (Correct):** 'Abundant' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means existing in large quantities; its opposite is 'scarce' (insufficient for demand or hard to find).
- **A — plentiful:** Meaning existing in or yielding great quantities (synonym: ample; positive tone), 'plentiful' is a synonym of 'abundant'.
- **C — copious:** Meaning abundant in supply or quantity (synonym: lavish; neutral tone), 'copious' implies large amounts.
- **D — profuse:** Meaning plentiful and overflowing (synonym: generous; neutral tone), 'profuse' indicates great abundance.
- **Exam Extra:** Compare 'scarce' (insufficient in quantity) with 'scarcely' (hardly/barely, an adverb tested in SSC grammar).

### Q308 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "substantial".

A. trivial
B. considerable
C. significant
D. hefty

**Answer:** A. trivial
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — trivial (Correct):** 'Substantial' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means of considerable importance, size, or worth; its opposite is 'trivial' (of little value or importance).
- **B — considerable:** Meaning large or great in size, value, or importance (synonym: major; positive/neutral tone), 'considerable' is a synonym of 'substantial'.
- **C — significant:** Meaning sufficiently great or important to be worthy of attention (synonym: notable; positive tone), 'significant' reinforces importance.
- **D — hefty:** Meaning solidly built and sturdy (synonym: solid; positive tone), 'hefty' implies weight and substance.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC antonym sets, 'substantial' pairs with 'insubstantial', 'trivial', 'paltry', and 'negligible'.

### Q309 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "daunt".

A. intimidate
B. discourage
C. encourage
D. unnerve

**Answer:** C. encourage
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — encourage (Correct):** 'Daunt' (verb, negative/neutral tone) means to make someone feel intimidated or apprehensive; its opposite is 'encourage' (give support, confidence, or hope).
- **A — intimidate:** Meaning to frighten or overawe someone (synonym: bully; negative tone), 'intimidate' is a synonym of 'daunt'.
- **B — discourage:** Meaning to cause someone to lose confidence or enthusiasm (synonym: dishearten; negative tone), 'discourage' aligns with 'daunt'.
- **D — unnerve:** Meaning to deprive of courage, hope, or confidence (synonym: dismay; negative tone), 'unnerve' weakens resolve rather than building it.
- **Exam Extra:** Pair to remember: 'daunt' (discourage/frighten) ↔ 'encourage/embolden/hearten'.

### Q310 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "plausible".

A. credible
B. reasonable
C. unbelievable
D. probable

**Answer:** C. unbelievable
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — unbelievable (Correct):** 'Plausible' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means seeming reasonable or probable; its opposite is 'unbelievable' (so unlikely or extreme as to be impossible to believe).
- **A — credible:** Meaning capable of being believed or trusted (synonym: convincing; positive tone), 'credible' is a synonym of 'plausible'.
- **B — reasonable:** Meaning having sound judgement and appearing sensible (synonym: logical; positive tone), 'reasonable' reinforces believability.
- **D — probable:** Meaning likely to happen or be true (synonym: likely; neutral tone), 'probable' aligns with plausibility.
- **Exam Extra:** Negative prefix forms of 'plausible' and 'credible' ('implausible', 'incredible') are often tested alongside 'unbelievable' and 'preposterous'.

### Q311 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "authentic".

A. spurious
B. genuine
C. reliable
D. legitimate

**Answer:** A. spurious
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — spurious (Correct):** 'Authentic' (adjective, positive tone) means of undisputed origin and genuine; its opposite is 'spurious' (not being what it purports to be; false or fake).
- **B — genuine:** Meaning truly what something is said to be (synonym: real; positive tone), 'genuine' is a synonym of 'authentic'.
- **C — reliable:** Meaning able to be trusted as accurate or true (synonym: dependable; positive tone), 'reliable' supports authenticity.
- **D — legitimate:** Meaning conforming to the law or rules and valid (synonym: valid; positive/neutral tone), 'legitimate' aligns with genuine authority.
- **Exam Extra:** 'Spurious' is one of SSC's favourite formal words for 'fake/bogus', paired against 'authentic', 'genuine', and 'bona fide'.

### Q312 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "adverse".

A. hostile
B. detrimental
C. unfavourable
D. favourable

**Answer:** D. favourable
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — favourable (Correct):** 'Adverse' (adjective, negative tone) means preventing success or development and harmful; its opposite is 'favourable' (expressing approval or providing an advantage).
- **A — hostile:** Meaning showing opposition or dislike (synonym: unfriendly; negative tone), 'hostile' is close in tone to 'adverse'.
- **B — detrimental:** Meaning harmful or damaging in effect (synonym: harmful; negative tone), 'detrimental' reinforces negative impact.
- **C — unfavourable:** Meaning unhelpful or inconvenient (synonym: disadvantageous; negative tone), 'unfavourable' is a direct synonym of 'adverse'.
- **Exam Extra:** Do not confuse 'adverse' (unfavourable/harmful, e.g., adverse weather) with 'averse' (unwilling or opposed, e.g., averse to risk).

### Q313 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "ephemeral".

A. fleeting
B. permanent
C. temporary
D. evanescent

**Answer:** B. permanent
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — permanent (Correct):** 'Ephemeral' (adjective, neutral tone) means lasting for a very short time; its opposite is 'permanent' (lasting or intended to last unchanged indefinitely).
- **A — fleeting:** Meaning lasting only for a short period (synonym: brief; neutral tone), 'fleeting' is a synonym of 'ephemeral'.
- **C — temporary:** Meaning lasting for only a limited period of time (synonym: short-lived; neutral tone), 'temporary' matches 'ephemeral'.
- **D — evanescent:** Meaning quickly fading from sight, memory, or existence (synonym: vanishing; neutral tone), 'evanescent' conveys brief duration.
- **Exam Extra:** High-frequency SSC antonym triad for 'ephemeral': 'permanent', 'perpetual', and 'eternal'.

### Q314 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "valiant".

A. cowardly
B. fearless
C. gallant
D. intrepid

**Answer:** A. cowardly
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — cowardly (Correct):** 'Valiant' (adjective, positive tone) means possessing or showing courage; its opposite is 'cowardly' (lacking courage and easily intimidated).
- **B — fearless:** Meaning feeling or showing no fear (synonym: brave; positive tone), 'fearless' is a synonym of 'valiant'.
- **C — gallant:** Meaning brave and heroic (synonym: chivalrous; positive tone), 'gallant' denotes noble courage.
- **D — intrepid:** Meaning fearless and adventurous (synonym: bold; positive tone), 'intrepid' implies resolute bravery.
- **Exam Extra:** Classic SSC antonym cluster: 'valiant/gallant/intrepid' ↔ 'cowardly/craven/timorous'.

### Q315 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "benevolent".

A. compassionate
B. malevolent
C. charitable
D. benign

**Answer:** B. malevolent
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — malevolent (Correct):** 'Benevolent' (adjective, positive tone) means well-meaning and kindly; its opposite is 'malevolent' (having or showing a wish to do evil to others).
- **A — compassionate:** Meaning showing sympathy, generosity, and concern for others (synonym: kind; positive tone), 'compassionate' is a synonym of 'benevolent'.
- **C — charitable:** Meaning characterized by generosity and willingness to help (synonym: philanthropic; positive tone), 'charitable' aligns with goodwill.
- **D — benign:** Meaning gentle, kindly, and not harmful (synonym: gracious; positive tone), 'benign' shares the 'bene-' root of goodness.
- **Exam Extra:** Latin prefix opposition: 'bene-' (good) vs 'male-' (bad) — also seen in 'benediction' (blessing) vs 'malediction' (curse).

### Q316 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "accurate".

A. precise
B. flawless
C. veracious
D. erroneous

**Answer:** D. erroneous
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — erroneous (Correct):** 'Accurate' (adjective, positive tone) means free from error and exact; its opposite is 'erroneous' (wrong or containing mistakes).
- **A — precise:** Meaning marked by exactness and accuracy of expression (synonym: exact; positive tone), 'precise' is a synonym of 'accurate'.
- **B — flawless:** Meaning completely free from faults or defects (synonym: spotless; positive tone), 'flawless' conveys perfection.
- **C — veracious:** Meaning faithful to fact or reality (synonym: true; positive tone), 'veracious' aligns with truthfulness and accuracy.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC exams, 'erroneous' and 'fallacious' are preferred formal antonyms for 'accurate' over simple prefix forms.

### Q317 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "exemplary".

A. commendable
B. laudable
C. inferior
D. meritorious

**Answer:** C. inferior
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — inferior (Correct):** 'Exemplary' (adjective, positive tone) means serving as a model of excellence; its opposite is 'inferior' (lower in rank, status, or quality).
- **A — commendable:** Meaning worthy of admiration and praise (synonym: admirable; positive tone), 'commendable' is a synonym of 'exemplary'.
- **B — laudable:** Meaning deserving high praise (synonym: praiseworthy; positive tone), 'laudable' expresses excellence.
- **D — meritorious:** Meaning worthy of respect and imitation (synonym: virtuous; positive tone), 'meritorious' indicates high merit.
- **Exam Extra:** Pair 'exemplary' (top standard) against 'inferior', 'deplorable', or 'substandard' in SSC antonym questions.

### Q318 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "abate".

A. subside
B. diminish
C. dwindle
D. increase

**Answer:** D. increase
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — increase (Correct):** 'Abate' (verb, neutral tone) means to become less intense or reduce; its opposite is 'increase' (become or make greater in size, amount, or intensity).
- **A — subside:** Meaning to become less intense, violent, or severe (synonym: calm; neutral tone), 'subside' is a synonym of 'abate'.
- **B — diminish:** Meaning to make or become less (synonym: reduce; neutral tone), 'diminish' matches the meaning of 'abate'.
- **C — dwindle:** Meaning to become gradually smaller or weaker (synonym: shrink; neutral tone), 'dwindle' denotes decline rather than growth.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember: 'abate' (subside/lessen) ↔ 'increase/intensify/escalate'.

### Q319 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "abhor".

A. detest
B. loathe
C. admire
D. execrate

**Answer:** C. admire
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — admire (Correct):** 'Abhor' (verb, strong negative tone) means to regard with disgust and hatred; its opposite is 'admire' (regard with respect or warm approval).
- **A — detest:** Meaning to dislike intensely (synonym: hate; negative tone), 'detest' is a synonym of 'abhor'.
- **B — loathe:** Meaning to feel intense dislike or disgust for (synonym: despise; negative tone), 'loathe' expresses strong aversion.
- **D — execrate:** Meaning to feel contempt or deep repugnance for (synonym: scorn; negative tone), 'execrate' is an extreme form of hatred.
- **Exam Extra:** Verb opposition to know: 'abhor/detest/loathe' ↔ 'admire/adore/esteem'.

### Q320 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "adamant".

A. obstinate
B. flexible
C. inflexible
D. resolute

**Answer:** B. flexible
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — flexible (Correct):** 'Adamant' (adjective, negative/emphatic tone) means refusing to change one's mind; its opposite is 'flexible' (willing to change or compromise).
- **A — obstinate:** Meaning stubbornly refusing to change one's opinion (synonym: stubborn; negative tone), 'obstinate' is a synonym of 'adamant'.
- **C — inflexible:** Meaning unwilling to change or compromise (synonym: rigid; negative tone), 'inflexible' reinforces unyieldingness.
- **D — resolute:** Meaning admirably purposeful and determined (synonym: firm; positive tone), 'resolute' implies steadfast firmness.
- **Exam Extra:** Antonyms of 'adamant' often tested by SSC include 'flexible', 'yielding', 'compliant', and 'pliable'.

### Q321 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "adversity".

A. prosperity
B. hardship
C. misfortune
D. affliction

**Answer:** A. prosperity
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — prosperity (Correct):** 'Adversity' (noun, negative tone) means difficulties or misfortune; its opposite is 'prosperity' (the state of being prosperous and flourishing).
- **B — hardship:** Meaning severe difficulty or suffering (synonym: distress; negative tone), 'hardship' is a synonym of 'adversity'.
- **C — misfortune:** Meaning bad luck or an unfortunate event (synonym: calamity; negative tone), 'misfortune' aligns with adversity.
- **D — affliction:** Meaning a state of great sorrow or physical discomfort (synonym: misery; negative tone), 'affliction' denotes suffering rather than success.
- **Exam Extra:** Proverbial contrast often echoed in SSC passages: 'Adversity tries friends; prosperity makes them.'

### Q322 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "amiable".

A. affable
B. hostile
C. cordial
D. genial

**Answer:** B. hostile
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — hostile (Correct):** 'Amiable' (adjective, positive tone) means friendly and pleasant; its opposite is 'hostile' (unfriendly and antagonistic).
- **A — affable:** Meaning pleasant, friendly, and easy to talk to (synonym: genial; positive tone), 'affable' is a synonym of 'amiable'.
- **C — cordial:** Meaning warm and friendly (synonym: gracious; positive tone), 'cordial' expresses pleasant goodwill.
- **D — genial:** Meaning cheerful and friendly (synonym: sociable; positive tone), 'genial' aligns with warmth rather than hostility.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember: 'amiable' (friendly/warm) ↔ 'hostile/antagonistic/surly'.

### Q323 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "candid".

A. frank
B. forthright
C. ingenuous
D. evasive

**Answer:** D. evasive
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — evasive (Correct):** 'Candid' (adjective, positive/neutral tone) means truthful and straightforward; its opposite is 'evasive' (tending to avoid commitment or direct answers).
- **A — frank:** Meaning open, honest, and direct in speech (synonym: blunt; neutral/positive tone), 'frank' is a synonym of 'candid'.
- **B — forthright:** Meaning direct and outspoken without hesitation (synonym: candid; positive tone), 'forthright' conveys openness.
- **C — ingenuous:** Meaning free from pretence and sincere (synonym: honest; positive tone), 'ingenuous' denotes innocent honesty.
- **Exam Extra:** Look out for 'candid' ↔ 'evasive/guarded/devious' in SSC Tier-1 and Tier-2 vocabulary sets.

### Q324 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "cease".

A. begin
B. halt
C. terminate
D. desist

**Answer:** A. begin
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — begin (Correct):** 'Cease' (verb, neutral tone) means to come or bring to an end; its opposite is 'begin' (start or perform the first part of an action).
- **B — halt:** Meaning to bring an activity or process to an end (synonym: stop; neutral tone), 'halt' is a synonym of 'cease'.
- **C — terminate:** Meaning to bring to an end (synonym: conclude; neutral tone), 'terminate' expresses stopping rather than starting.
- **D — desist:** Meaning to stop doing something (synonym: refrain; neutral tone), 'desist' means to cease an action.
- **Exam Extra:** Standard SSC antonym pair: 'cease/terminate/halt' ↔ 'begin/commence/initiate'.

### Q325 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "coherent".

A. logical
B. articulate
C. confused
D. consistent

**Answer:** C. confused
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — confused (Correct):** 'Coherent' (adjective, positive tone) means logical, consistent, and clear; its opposite is 'confused' (lacking order or clarity and hard to understand).
- **A — logical:** Meaning clearly reasoned and well-structured (synonym: rational; positive tone), 'logical' is a synonym of 'coherent'.
- **B — articulate:** Meaning able to speak fluently and clearly (synonym: eloquent; positive tone), 'articulate' conveys clarity of expression.
- **D — consistent:** Meaning forming a united whole without contradiction (synonym: steady; positive tone), 'consistent' reinforces coherence.
- **Exam Extra:** Notice the pair: 'coherent' (logical/ordered) ↔ 'confused/incoherent/muddled'.

### Q326 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "concise".

A. brief
B. succinct
C. lengthy
D. terse

**Answer:** C. lengthy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — lengthy (Correct):** 'Concise' (adjective, positive tone) means brief and to the point; its opposite is 'lengthy' (of considerable or unusual length, especially tediously so).
- **A — brief:** Meaning expressed in very few words (synonym: short; neutral/positive tone), 'brief' is a synonym of 'concise'.
- **B — succinct:** Meaning briefly and clearly expressed (synonym: compact; positive tone), 'succinct' denotes brevity.
- **D — terse:** Meaning sparing in the use of words and abrupt (synonym: curt; neutral tone), 'terse' means brief rather than long.
- **Exam Extra:** High-frequency SSC antonyms for 'concise' are 'lengthy', 'verbose', 'wordy', and 'prolix'.

### Q327 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "diligent".

A. lazy
B. industrious
C. conscientious
D. assiduous

**Answer:** A. lazy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — lazy (Correct):** 'Diligent' (adjective, positive tone) means hardworking and conscientious; its opposite is 'lazy' (unwilling to work or use energy).
- **B — industrious:** Meaning characterized by hard work and perseverance (synonym: hardworking; positive tone), 'industrious' is a synonym of 'diligent'.
- **C — conscientious:** Meaning thorough and careful in duty (synonym: dedicated; positive tone), 'conscientious' reflects steady effort.
- **D — assiduous:** Meaning attentive and persevering (synonym: steadfast; positive tone), 'assiduous' aligns with hard work.
- **Exam Extra:** Classic SSC antonym set: 'diligent/industrious/assiduous' ↔ 'lazy/indolent/slothful'.

### Q328 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "elated".

A. jubilant
B. dejected
C. ecstatic
D. euphoric

**Answer:** B. dejected
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — dejected (Correct):** 'Elated' (adjective, positive tone) means extremely happy and excited; its opposite is 'dejected' (sad and depressed; dispirited).
- **A — jubilant:** Meaning feeling or expressing great happiness and triumph (synonym: overjoyed; positive tone), 'jubilant' is a synonym of 'elated'.
- **C — ecstatic:** Meaning feeling intense happiness and excitement (synonym: thrilled; positive tone), 'ecstatic' conveys extreme joy.
- **D — euphoric:** Meaning characterized by intense excitement and happiness (synonym: blissful; positive tone), 'euphoric' is the opposite of gloom.
- **Exam Extra:** Latin 'de-' (down) + 'jacere' (to throw) = cast down in spirits, contrasting directly with 'elated' (lifted up in spirits).

### Q329 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "novice".

A. apprentice
B. neophyte
C. amateur
D. veteran

**Answer:** D. veteran
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — veteran (Correct):** 'Novice' (noun, neutral tone) means a person new to or inexperienced in a field or situation; its opposite is 'veteran' (a person who has had long experience in a particular field).
- **A — apprentice:** Meaning a person who is learning a trade from a skilled employer (synonym: trainee; neutral tone), 'apprentice' is close in meaning to 'novice'.
- **B — neophyte:** Meaning a person new to a subject, skill, or belief (synonym: beginner; neutral tone), 'neophyte' is a direct synonym of 'novice'.
- **C — amateur:** Meaning a person who engages in an activity without professional training (synonym: non-professional; neutral tone), 'amateur' contrasts with a seasoned expert.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC synonym cluster for 'novice': 'neophyte', 'tyro', 'rookie', and 'apprentice'; antonym: 'veteran' or 'maestro'.

### Q330 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "optimist".

A. pessimist
B. idealist
C. visionary
D. realist

**Answer:** A. pessimist
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — pessimist (Correct):** 'Optimist' (noun, positive tone) means a person who tends to be hopeful and confident about the future; its opposite is 'pessimist' (a person who tends to see the worst aspect of things).
- **B — idealist:** Meaning a person who is guided more by ideals than practical considerations (synonym: dreamer; neutral/positive tone), 'idealist' is not the direct opposite of hopefulness.
- **C — visionary:** Meaning a person with original ideas about the future (synonym: prophet; positive tone), 'visionary' implies foresight rather than gloom.
- **D — realist:** Meaning a person who accepts a situation as it is and deals with it practically (synonym: pragmatist; neutral tone), 'realist' sits between optimism and pessimism.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'optimus' (best) vs 'pessimus' (worst) — remember the classic SSC one-word pair: one who looks at the bright side vs dark side.

### Q331 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "misanthrope".

A. recluse
B. cynic
C. humanitarian
D. bigot

**Answer:** C. humanitarian
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — humanitarian (Correct):** 'Misanthrope' (noun, negative tone) means a person who dislikes humankind and avoids human society; its opposite is 'humanitarian' (a person who seeks to promote human welfare).
- **A — recluse:** Meaning a person who lives a solitary life and avoids other people (synonym: hermit; neutral/negative tone), 'recluse' shares the antisocial tendency of a misanthrope.
- **B — cynic:** Meaning a person who believes people are motivated purely by self-interest (synonym: skeptic; negative tone), 'cynic' aligns with distrust of humanity.
- **D — bigot:** Meaning a person who is obstinately or intolerantly devoted to their own prejudices (synonym: fanatic; negative tone), 'bigot' reflects intolerance rather than love for humanity.
- **Exam Extra:** Greek root contrast: 'miso-' (hate) vs 'philo-' (love); 'misanthrope' is paired against 'philanthropist' or 'humanitarian' in SSC exams.

### Q332 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "gregarious".

A. sociable
B. convivial
C. outgoing
D. reclusive

**Answer:** D. reclusive
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — reclusive (Correct):** 'Gregarious' (adjective, positive tone) means fond of company and sociable; its opposite is 'reclusive' (avoiding the company of other people and solitary).
- **A — sociable:** Meaning willing to talk and engage in activities with other people (synonym: friendly; positive tone), 'sociable' is a synonym of 'gregarious'.
- **B — convivial:** Meaning friendly, lively, and enjoyable in company (synonym: jovial; positive tone), 'convivial' reflects love of socializing.
- **C — outgoing:** Meaning friendly and socially confident (synonym: extroverted; positive tone), 'outgoing' implies seeking social interaction.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'grex' (flock/herd), literally meaning flocking together; SSC synonyms are 'sociable' and 'convivial'.

### Q333 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "obsolete".

A. antiquated
B. contemporary
C. archaic
D. passé

**Answer:** B. contemporary
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — contemporary (Correct):** 'Obsolete' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means out of date and no longer produced or used; its opposite is 'contemporary' (belonging to or occurring in the present; modern).
- **A — antiquated:** Meaning very old or old-fashioned (synonym: outdated; negative/neutral tone), 'antiquated' is a synonym of 'obsolete'.
- **C — archaic:** Meaning very old or old-fashioned and no longer in common use (synonym: ancient; neutral tone), 'archaic' shares the sense of being outdated.
- **D — passé:** Meaning no longer fashionable or widely used (synonym: outmoded; negative tone), 'passé' conveys obsolescence rather than modernity.
- **Exam Extra:** High-frequency SSC synonyms for 'obsolete': 'outdated', 'antiquated', and 'archaic'; antonyms: 'modern', 'current', 'contemporary'.

### Q334 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "tranquil".

A. serene
B. placid
C. halcyon
D. turbulent

**Answer:** D. turbulent
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — turbulent (Correct):** 'Tranquil' (adjective, positive tone) means free from disturbance and calm; its opposite is 'turbulent' (characterized by conflict, disorder, or confusion).
- **A — serene:** Meaning calm, peaceful, and untroubled (synonym: calm; positive tone), 'serene' is a synonym of 'tranquil'.
- **B — placid:** Meaning not easily upset or excited and calm (synonym: tranquil; positive/neutral tone), 'placid' denotes peaceful stillness.
- **C — halcyon:** Meaning denoting a period of time that was idyllically happy and peaceful (synonym: peaceful; positive tone), 'halcyon' implies calm rather than turmoil.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember SSC synonyms for 'tranquil': 'serene', 'placid', and 'halcyon', and antonyms: 'turbulent', 'tumultuous', and 'agitated'.

### Q335 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "zealous".

A. ardent
B. apathetic
C. enthusiastic
D. fervent

**Answer:** B. apathetic
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — apathetic (Correct):** 'Zealous' (adjective, positive/emphatic tone) means having or showing great energy or enthusiasm in pursuit of a cause; its opposite is 'apathetic' (showing or feeling no interest, enthusiasm, or concern).
- **A — ardent:** Meaning enthusiastic or passionate (synonym: passionate; positive tone), 'ardent' is a synonym of 'zealous'.
- **C — enthusiastic:** Meaning having or showing intense and eager enjoyment or interest (synonym: eager; positive tone), 'enthusiastic' matches high zeal.
- **D — fervent:** Meaning showing strong and passionate feelings (synonym: fervent; positive tone), 'fervent' conveys intense devotion rather than indifference.
- **Exam Extra:** Related nouns: 'zeal' (great enthusiasm) and 'zealot' (a fanatical follower); antonym 'apathy' (lack of interest).

### Q336 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "ambiguous".

A. vague
B. equivocal
C. explicit
D. obscure

**Answer:** C. explicit
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — explicit (Correct):** 'Ambiguous' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means open to more than one interpretation and unclear; its opposite is 'explicit' (stated clearly and in detail, leaving no room for confusion).
- **A — vague:** Meaning of uncertain, indefinite, or unclear character (synonym: indistinct; negative/neutral tone), 'vague' is a synonym of 'ambiguous'.
- **B — equivocal:** Meaning open to more than one interpretation and intentionally unclear (synonym: cryptic; negative tone), 'equivocal' shares the double-meaning nuance.
- **D — obscure:** Meaning not clearly expressed or hard to understand (synonym: murky; negative tone), 'obscure' denotes lack of clarity.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'ambi-' (both ways) + 'agere' (to drive); SSC antonym pair: 'ambiguous/equivocal' ↔ 'explicit/unambiguous/lucid'.

### Q337 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "complacent".

A. dissatisfied
B. smug
C. contented
D. pleased

**Answer:** A. dissatisfied
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — dissatisfied (Correct):** 'Complacent' (adjective, negative tone) means showing smug or uncritical satisfaction with oneself or one's achievements; its opposite is 'dissatisfied' (not content or happy with something).
- **B — smug:** Meaning contented to a fault with oneself or one's situation (synonym: self-satisfied; negative tone), 'smug' is a synonym of 'complacent'.
- **C — contented:** Meaning in a state of peaceful happiness and satisfaction (synonym: satisfied; positive tone), 'contented' implies satisfaction rather than discontent.
- **D — pleased:** Meaning showing satisfaction with one's own merits (synonym: self-satisfied; negative tone), 'pleased' aligns with satisfaction.
- **Exam Extra:** Classic SSC confusion pair: 'complacent' (smug/self-satisfied) vs 'complaisant' (willing to please others/obliging).

### Q338 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "conspicuous".

A. prominent
B. obscure
C. evident
D. blatant

**Answer:** B. obscure
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — obscure (Correct):** 'Conspicuous' (adjective, neutral tone) means standing out so as to be clearly visible; its opposite is 'obscure' (not discovered or known about; hidden from view).
- **A — prominent:** Meaning important, famous, and easily seen (synonym: noticeable; positive/neutral tone), 'prominent' is a synonym of 'conspicuous'.
- **C — evident:** Meaning plain or obvious and clearly seen or understood (synonym: apparent; neutral tone), 'evident' conveys visibility.
- **D — blatant:** Meaning conspicuously or obviously offensive or glaring (synonym: overt; negative/neutral tone), 'blatant' means unmistakably noticeable.
- **Exam Extra:** Pair to remember: 'conspicuous' (easily noticed) ↔ 'obscure/inconspicuous/hidden'.

### Q339 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "copious".

A. meagre
B. ample
C. profuse
D. plentiful

**Answer:** A. meagre
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — meagre (Correct):** 'Copious' (adjective, neutral/positive tone) means abundant in supply or quantity; its opposite is 'meagre' (lacking in quantity or quality).
- **B — ample:** Meaning enough or more than enough and plentiful (synonym: abundant; positive tone), 'ample' is a synonym of 'copious'.
- **C — profuse:** Meaning very plentiful and abundant (synonym: lavish; neutral tone), 'profuse' denotes overflowing supply.
- **D — plentiful:** Meaning existing in large quantities (synonym: generous; positive tone), 'plentiful' reinforces abundance.
- **Exam Extra:** Top SSC antonym pair: 'copious/profuse/abundant' ↔ 'meagre/scanty/sparse'.

### Q340 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "futile".

A. vain
B. abortive
C. fruitful
D. worthless

**Answer:** C. fruitful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — fruitful (Correct):** 'Futile' (adjective, negative tone) means incapable of producing any useful result and pointless; its opposite is 'fruitful' (producing good or helpful results; productive).
- **A — vain:** Meaning producing no result and useless (synonym: pointless; negative tone), 'vain' is a synonym of 'futile'.
- **B — abortive:** Meaning failing to produce the intended result (synonym: fruitless; negative tone), 'abortive' implies unsuccessful effort.
- **D — worthless:** Meaning having no value or usefulness (synonym: ineffective; negative tone), 'worthless' reinforces lack of useful outcome.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC synonyms for 'futile' include 'vain', 'bootless', and 'abortive'; antonyms include 'fruitful', 'productive', and 'efficacious'.

### Q341 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "impetuous".

A. rash
B. impulsive
C. headstrong
D. cautious

**Answer:** D. cautious
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — cautious (Correct):** 'Impetuous' (adjective, negative tone) means acting or done quickly and without thought or care; its opposite is 'cautious' (careful to avoid potential problems or dangers).
- **A — rash:** Meaning acting or done without careful consideration of possible consequences (synonym: reckless; negative tone), 'rash' is a synonym of 'impetuous'.
- **B — impulsive:** Meaning acting or done without forethought (synonym: spontaneous; negative/neutral tone), 'impulsive' conveys sudden unthinking action.
- **C — headstrong:** Meaning energetically wilful and determined to have one's own way (synonym: obstinate; negative tone), 'headstrong' implies rashness rather than caution.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC exams, 'impetuous' pairs with 'rash/impulsive/headstrong' as synonyms and 'cautious/circumspect/prudent' as antonyms.

### Q342 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "indigenous".

A. native
B. endemic
C. foreign
D. domestic

**Answer:** C. foreign
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — foreign (Correct):** 'Indigenous' (adjective, neutral tone) means originating or occurring naturally in a particular place; its opposite is 'foreign' (of, from, in, or characteristic of a country or language other than one's own).
- **A — native:** Meaning associated with the country or region of one's birth (synonym: local; neutral tone), 'native' is a synonym of 'indigenous'.
- **B — endemic:** Meaning native and restricted to a certain place (synonym: localized; neutral tone), 'endemic' denotes local origin.
- **D — domestic:** Meaning belonging to or existing inside a particular place or country (synonym: home-grown; neutral tone), 'domestic' aligns with native origin.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC frequently pairs 'indigenous' with 'native' and 'endemic' as synonyms, and 'foreign', 'alien', or 'exotic' as antonyms.

### Q343 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "insipid".

A. bland
B. vapid
C. tasteless
D. flavourful

**Answer:** D. flavourful
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — flavourful (Correct):** 'Insipid' (adjective, negative tone) means lacking flavour, vigour, or interest; its opposite is 'flavourful' (full of flavour; tasty and engaging).
- **A — bland:** Meaning lacking strong features or characteristics and dull (synonym: tasteless; negative tone), 'bland' is a synonym of 'insipid'.
- **B — vapid:** Meaning offering nothing that is stimulating or challenging (synonym: dull; negative tone), 'vapid' conveys flatness and lack of zest.
- **C — tasteless:** Meaning lacking flavour or interest (synonym: uninteresting; negative tone), 'tasteless' reinforces the meaning of 'insipid'.
- **Exam Extra:** Literally 'without taste' (Latin 'in-' + 'sapidus' = tasty); in SSC, 'insipid' can describe either bland food or dull writing, opposed to 'flavourful' or 'zesty'.

### Q344 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "laconic".

A. verbose
B. terse
C. succinct
D. pithy

**Answer:** A. verbose
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — verbose (Correct):** 'Laconic' (adjective, neutral/positive tone) means using very few words; its opposite is 'verbose' (using or expressed in more words than are needed).
- **B — terse:** Meaning using few words and sometimes abrupt (synonym: brief; neutral tone), 'terse' is a synonym of 'laconic'.
- **C — succinct:** Meaning briefly and clearly expressed (synonym: concise; positive tone), 'succinct' denotes economy of words.
- **D — pithy:** Meaning concise and forcefully expressive (synonym: compact; positive tone), 'pithy' aligns with brevity rather than wordiness.
- **Exam Extra:** Named after Laconia (Sparta), whose people were famous for brief, pithy replies; antonym 'verbose' or 'loquacious'.

### Q345 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "mundane".

A. ordinary
B. extraordinary
C. prosaic
D. commonplace

**Answer:** B. extraordinary
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — extraordinary (Correct):** 'Mundane' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means lacking interest or excitement and dull or ordinary; its opposite is 'extraordinary' (very unusual or remarkable).
- **A — ordinary:** Meaning with no special or distinctive features (synonym: commonplace; neutral tone), 'ordinary' is a synonym of 'mundane'.
- **C — prosaic:** Meaning dull, ordinary, and lacking excitement (synonym: uninspiring; negative tone), 'prosaic' reinforces everyday dullness.
- **D — commonplace:** Meaning found or happening often and lacking distinction (synonym: routine; neutral tone), 'commonplace' aligns with 'mundane'.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'mundus' (world), initially meaning 'worldly/earthly' before shifting to 'everyday/ordinary'; antonyms include 'extraordinary' and 'heavenly'.

### Q346 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "nebulous".

A. distinct
B. hazy
C. vague
D. murky

**Answer:** A. distinct
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — distinct (Correct):** 'Nebulous' (adjective, negative/neutral tone) means in the form of a cloud or haze; vague and ill-defined; its opposite is 'distinct' (recognizably different and clearly defined).
- **B — hazy:** Meaning covered by a haze and unclear (synonym: indistinct; negative/neutral tone), 'hazy' is a synonym of 'nebulous'.
- **C — vague:** Meaning uncertain or unclear in character or meaning (synonym: ambiguous; negative tone), 'vague' conveys lack of clear outline.
- **D — murky:** Meaning not clearly visible or understood and cloudy (synonym: obscure; negative tone), 'murky' matches fogginess rather than clarity.
- **Exam Extra:** From Latin 'nebula' (cloud/mist); SSC synonyms are 'hazy', 'vague', and 'murky', and antonyms are 'distinct' and 'clear'.

### Q347 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "nefarious".

A. wicked
B. virtuous
C. heinous
D. villainous

**Answer:** B. virtuous
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — virtuous (Correct):** 'Nefarious' (adjective, strong negative tone) means wicked or criminal; its opposite is 'virtuous' (having or showing high moral standards).
- **A — wicked:** Meaning evil or morally wrong (synonym: sinful; negative tone), 'wicked' is a synonym of 'nefarious'.
- **C — heinous:** Meaning utterly odious or wicked (synonym: monstrous; strong negative tone), 'heinous' implies extreme immorality.
- **D — villainous:** Meaning extremely wicked or villainous (synonym: depraved; strong negative tone), 'villainous' aligns with criminal wickedness.
- **Exam Extra:** Rooted in Latin 'nefas' (wrong/crime); top SSC synonyms for 'nefarious' are 'wicked', 'heinous', and 'iniquitous'.

### Q348 · Antonyms

Select the most appropriate antonym of "obdurate".

A. stubborn
B. obstinate
C. unbending
