D. The certificate would be issued by the officer.

**Answer:** B. The certificate will be issued by the officer.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The certificate will be issued by the officer. (Correct):** Simple Future active ('will issue') changes to 'will/shall + be + V3' in passive voice, giving 'will be issued'.
- **A — The certificate is issued by the officer.:** Using 'is issued' shifts Simple Future to Simple Present passive.
- **C — The certificate will have been issued by the officer.:** Using 'will have been issued' shifts Simple Future to Future Perfect passive.
- **D — The certificate would be issued by the officer.:** Using 'would be issued' alters the future modal 'will' to conditional/past 'would'.
- **Exam Extra:** Simple Future Passive formula: Object + will/shall + be + V3 + by + Subject. Never replace 'will' with 'would' unless the main clause is in past reported speech.

### Q520 · Simple Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The committee will announce the results tomorrow."

A. The results will be announced by the committee tomorrow.
B. The results are announced by the committee tomorrow.
C. The results will be announcing by the committee tomorrow.
D. The results will have been announced by the committee tomorrow.

**Answer:** A. The results will be announced by the committee tomorrow.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — The results will be announced by the committee tomorrow. (Correct):** Simple Future ('will announce') converts to 'will be announced' when 'The results' becomes the passive subject.
- **B — The results are announced by the committee tomorrow.:** Using 'are announced' shifts Simple Future to Simple Present passive.
- **C — The results will be announcing by the committee tomorrow.:** Using 'will be announcing' uses active Future Continuous instead of passive Simple Future ('will be + V3').
- **D — The results will have been announced by the committee tomorrow.:** Using 'will have been announced' shifts Simple Future to Future Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Future Continuous active ('will be + V-ing') is a classic distractor for Simple Future passive ('will be + V3'). Check the verb ending carefully.

### Q521 · Simple Tenses

Select the correct active form of: "A new library will be built in this town by the council."

A. The council would build a new library in this town.
B. The council is building a new library in this town.
C. The council will build a new library in this town.
D. The council will have built a new library in this town.

**Answer:** C. The council will build a new library in this town.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The council will build a new library in this town. (Correct):** 'will be built' is Simple Future passive, so its active counterpart uses 'will + V1' ('will build') with 'The council' as the subject.
- **A — The council would build a new library in this town.:** Using 'would build' changes the future modal 'will' to 'would'.
- **B — The council is building a new library in this town.:** Using 'is building' shifts Simple Future passive to Present Continuous active.
- **D — The council will have built a new library in this town.:** Using 'will have built' shifts Simple Future passive to Future Perfect active.
- **Exam Extra:** To convert 'will be + V3' to active voice, drop 'be' and restore V3 to its base V1 form after 'will'.

### Q522 · Simple Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The jury did not accept the lawyer's argument."

A. The lawyer's argument is not accepted by the jury.
B. The lawyer's argument had not been accepted by the jury.
C. The lawyer's argument were not accepted by the jury.
D. The lawyer's argument was not accepted by the jury.

**Answer:** D. The lawyer's argument was not accepted by the jury.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The lawyer's argument was not accepted by the jury. (Correct):** Negative Simple Past ('did not accept') changes to 'was/were + not + V3'. Since 'The lawyer's argument' is singular, 'was not accepted' is right.
- **A — The lawyer's argument is not accepted by the jury.:** Using 'is not accepted' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **B — The lawyer's argument had not been accepted by the jury.:** Using 'had not been accepted' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **C — The lawyer's argument were not accepted by the jury.:** Using 'were not accepted' creates a number disagreement with the singular subject 'argument'.
- **Exam Extra:** Active 'did not + V1' always converts to 'was/were + not + V3' in passive voice; 'did' never appears in a passive assertive sentence.

### Q523 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "They are repairing the bridge."

A. The bridge is repaired by them.
B. The bridge was being repaired by them.
C. The bridge is being repaired by them.
D. The bridge has been repaired by them.

**Answer:** C. The bridge is being repaired by them.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The bridge is being repaired by them. (Correct):** Present Continuous active ('are repairing') becomes 'is/am/are + being + V3'. Since 'The bridge' is singular, 'is being repaired' is correct.
- **A — The bridge is repaired by them.:** Using 'is repaired' omits 'being', changing Present Continuous to Simple Present passive.
- **B — The bridge was being repaired by them.:** Using 'was being repaired' shifts Present Continuous to Past Continuous passive.
- **D — The bridge has been repaired by them.:** Using 'has been repaired' shifts Present Continuous to Present Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Present Continuous Passive formula: Object + is/am/are + being + V3 + by + Subject. The word 'being' is mandatory to preserve continuous aspect.

### Q524 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The workers are painting the bridge."

A. The bridge was being painted by the workers.
B. The bridge has been painted by the workers.
C. The bridge is painted by the workers.
D. The bridge is being painted by the workers.

**Answer:** D. The bridge is being painted by the workers.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The bridge is being painted by the workers. (Correct):** Present Continuous active ('are painting') transforms into 'is/am/are + being + V3'. With singular 'The bridge', 'is being painted' is correct.
- **A — The bridge was being painted by the workers.:** Using 'was being painted' changes Present Continuous to Past Continuous passive.
- **B — The bridge has been painted by the workers.:** Using 'has been painted' changes Present Continuous to Present Perfect passive.
- **C — The bridge is painted by the workers.:** Using 'is painted' omits 'being', shifting Present Continuous to Simple Present passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Do not confuse 'being + V3' (Continuous passive) with 'been + V3' (Perfect passive).

### Q525 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The chef is preparing special dishes for the guests."

A. Special dishes are being prepared by the chef for the guests.
B. Special dishes is being prepared by the chef for the guests.
C. Special dishes were being prepared by the chef for the guests.
D. Special dishes are prepared by the chef for the guests.

**Answer:** A. Special dishes are being prepared by the chef for the guests.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Special dishes are being prepared by the chef for the guests. (Correct):** Present Continuous active ('is preparing') shifts to 'are being prepared' because the new subject 'Special dishes' is plural.
- **B — Special dishes is being prepared by the chef for the guests.:** Using 'is being prepared' violates subject-verb agreement with the plural subject 'Special dishes'.
- **C — Special dishes were being prepared by the chef for the guests.:** Using 'were being prepared' shifts Present Continuous to Past Continuous passive.
- **D — Special dishes are prepared by the chef for the guests.:** Using 'are prepared' omits 'being', reducing Present Continuous to Simple Present passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Even when the active subject ('The chef') is singular, the passive auxiliary must match the plural new subject ('Special dishes -> are').

### Q526 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct active form of: "A documentary is being filmed by the crew in Ladakh."

A. The crew filmed a documentary in Ladakh.
B. The crew is filming a documentary in Ladakh.
C. The crew was filming a documentary in Ladakh.
D. The crew has filmed a documentary in Ladakh.

**Answer:** B. The crew is filming a documentary in Ladakh.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The crew is filming a documentary in Ladakh. (Correct):** 'is being filmed' is Present Continuous passive, so the active form requires Present Continuous 'is filming' with the singular collective noun 'The crew'.
- **A — The crew filmed a documentary in Ladakh.:** Using 'filmed' changes Present Continuous passive to Simple Past active.
- **C — The crew was filming a documentary in Ladakh.:** Using 'was filming' shifts Present Continuous passive to Past Continuous active.
- **D — The crew has filmed a documentary in Ladakh.:** Using 'has filmed' shifts Present Continuous passive to Present Perfect active.
- **Exam Extra:** When converting 'is/are + being + V3' back to active voice, drop 'being' and change V3 into the '-ing' form.

### Q527 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The students were decorating the auditorium."

A. The auditorium was being decorated by the students.
B. The auditorium were being decorated by the students.
C. The auditorium is being decorated by the students.
D. The auditorium was decorated by the students.

**Answer:** A. The auditorium was being decorated by the students.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — The auditorium was being decorated by the students. (Correct):** Past Continuous active ('were decorating') becomes 'was/were + being + V3'. Since 'The auditorium' is singular, 'was being decorated' is correct.
- **B — The auditorium were being decorated by the students.:** Using 'were being decorated' pairs a plural verb 'were' with the singular subject 'The auditorium'.
- **C — The auditorium is being decorated by the students.:** Using 'is being decorated' shifts Past Continuous to Present Continuous passive.
- **D — The auditorium was decorated by the students.:** Using 'was decorated' omits 'being', changing Past Continuous to Simple Past passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Past Continuous Passive formula: Object + was/were + being + V3 + by + Subject.

### Q528 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The police were questioning the suspects."

A. The suspects was being questioned by the police.
B. The suspects had been questioned by the police.
C. The suspects were being questioned by the police.
D. The suspects are being questioned by the police.

**Answer:** C. The suspects were being questioned by the police.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The suspects were being questioned by the police. (Correct):** Past Continuous active ('were questioning') transforms into 'were being questioned' to agree with the plural subject 'The suspects'.
- **A — The suspects was being questioned by the police.:** Using 'was being questioned' disagrees in number with the plural subject 'The suspects'.
- **B — The suspects had been questioned by the police.:** Using 'had been questioned' shifts Past Continuous to Past Perfect passive.
- **D — The suspects are being questioned by the police.:** Using 'are being questioned' shifts Past Continuous to Present Continuous passive.
- **Exam Extra:** 'The police' is treated as plural in English, and here the passive subject 'The suspects' is also plural, requiring 'were'.

### Q529 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct active form of: "The wounded soldiers were being treated by the doctors."

A. The doctors treated the wounded soldiers.
B. The doctors are treating the wounded soldiers.
C. The doctors had treated the wounded soldiers.
D. The doctors were treating the wounded soldiers.

**Answer:** D. The doctors were treating the wounded soldiers.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The doctors were treating the wounded soldiers. (Correct):** 'were being treated' is Past Continuous passive, so its active equivalent is Past Continuous 'were treating' with 'The doctors' as subject.
- **A — The doctors treated the wounded soldiers.:** Using 'treated' reduces Past Continuous passive to Simple Past active.
- **B — The doctors are treating the wounded soldiers.:** Using 'are treating' shifts Past Continuous passive to Present Continuous active.
- **C — The doctors had treated the wounded soldiers.:** Using 'had treated' changes Past Continuous passive to Past Perfect active.
- **Exam Extra:** Notice that Future Continuous ('will be V-ing') and Perfect Continuous tenses do not take standard passive forms in SSC grammar.

### Q530 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The municipality is not collecting the garbage today."

A. The garbage is not collected by the municipality today.
B. The garbage is not being collected by the municipality today.
C. The garbage was not being collected by the municipality today.
D. The garbage has not been collected by the municipality today.

**Answer:** B. The garbage is not being collected by the municipality today.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The garbage is not being collected by the municipality today. (Correct):** Negative Present Continuous ('is not collecting') converts to 'is/am/are + not + being + V3', yielding 'is not being collected'.
- **A — The garbage is not collected by the municipality today.:** Using 'is not collected' drops 'being', shifting Present Continuous to Simple Present passive.
- **C — The garbage was not being collected by the municipality today.:** Using 'was not being collected' shifts Present Continuous to Past Continuous passive.
- **D — The garbage has not been collected by the municipality today.:** Using 'has not been collected' shifts Present Continuous to Present Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** In negative continuous passives, 'not' always comes directly after the first auxiliary ('is/are/was/were') and before 'being'.

### Q531 · Continuous Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "She was not reading the novel."

A. The novel was not read by her.
B. The novel is not being read by her.
C. The novel was not being read by her.
D. The novel had not been read by her.

**Answer:** C. The novel was not being read by her.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The novel was not being read by her. (Correct):** Negative Past Continuous ('was not reading') changes to 'was not being read' for the singular subject 'The novel'.
- **A — The novel was not read by her.:** Using 'was not read' drops 'being', turning Past Continuous into Simple Past passive.
- **B — The novel is not being read by her.:** Using 'is not being read' shifts Past Continuous to Present Continuous passive.
- **D — The novel had not been read by her.:** Using 'had not been read' shifts Past Continuous to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** All three forms of 'read' share the same spelling, though the V2/V3 forms are pronounced like 'red'.

### Q532 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The team has completed the project."

A. The project had been completed by the team.
B. The project has been completed by the team.
C. The project was completed by the team.
D. The project is being completed by the team.

**Answer:** B. The project has been completed by the team.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The project has been completed by the team. (Correct):** Present Perfect active ('has completed') changes to 'has/have + been + V3'. With singular 'The project', 'has been completed' is correct.
- **A — The project had been completed by the team.:** Using 'had been completed' shifts Present Perfect to Past Perfect passive.
- **C — The project was completed by the team.:** Using 'was completed' shifts Present Perfect to Simple Past passive.
- **D — The project is being completed by the team.:** Using 'is being completed' shifts Present Perfect to Present Continuous passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Present Perfect Passive formula: Object + has/have + been + V3 + by + Subject.

### Q533 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The publisher has released three new editions."

A. Three new editions have been released by the publisher.
B. Three new editions has been released by the publisher.
C. Three new editions were released by the publisher.
D. Three new editions had been released by the publisher.

**Answer:** A. Three new editions have been released by the publisher.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Three new editions have been released by the publisher. (Correct):** Present Perfect active ('has released') converts to 'have been released' because the new passive subject 'Three new editions' is plural.
- **B — Three new editions has been released by the publisher.:** Using 'has been released' creates a subject-verb agreement error with plural 'Three new editions'.
- **C — Three new editions were released by the publisher.:** Using 'were released' shifts Present Perfect to Simple Past passive.
- **D — Three new editions had been released by the publisher.:** Using 'had been released' shifts Present Perfect to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** When the active subject is singular ('The publisher -> has') and the object is plural ('Three new editions -> have'), switching 'has' to 'have' is a high-frequency SSC test point.

### Q534 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct active form of: "The lost wallet has been found by a passerby."

A. A passerby found the lost wallet.
B. A passerby had found the lost wallet.
C. A passerby is finding the lost wallet.
D. A passerby has found the lost wallet.

**Answer:** D. A passerby has found the lost wallet.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — A passerby has found the lost wallet. (Correct):** 'has been found' is Present Perfect passive, so its active form uses Present Perfect 'has found' with 'A passerby' as the subject.
- **A — A passerby found the lost wallet.:** Using 'found' converts Present Perfect passive into Simple Past active.
- **B — A passerby had found the lost wallet.:** Using 'had found' shifts Present Perfect passive to Past Perfect active.
- **C — A passerby is finding the lost wallet.:** Using 'is finding' shifts Present Perfect passive to Present Continuous active.
- **Exam Extra:** To convert a Perfect passive ('has/have/had + been + V3') to active voice, simply remove 'been'.

### Q535 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The workers had finished the repairs."

A. The repairs have been finished by the workers.
B. The repairs were finished by the workers.
C. The repairs had been finished by the workers.
D. The repairs had finished by the workers.

**Answer:** C. The repairs had been finished by the workers.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The repairs had been finished by the workers. (Correct):** Past Perfect active ('had finished') converts to 'had + been + V3' in passive voice, giving 'had been finished'.
- **A — The repairs have been finished by the workers.:** Using 'have been finished' shifts Past Perfect to Present Perfect passive.
- **B — The repairs were finished by the workers.:** Using 'were finished' shifts Past Perfect to Simple Past passive.
- **D — The repairs had finished by the workers.:** Using 'had finished' omits 'been', leaving the verb in active voice.
- **Exam Extra:** Past Perfect Passive formula: Object + had + been + V3 + by + Subject ('had' stays the same for both singular and plural subjects).

### Q536 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The chef had baked the cake before the guests arrived."

A. The cake had been baked by the chef before the guests arrived.
B. The cake was baked by the chef before the guests arrived.
C. The cake has been baked by the chef before the guests arrived.
D. The cake had been baking by the chef before the guests arrived.

**Answer:** A. The cake had been baked by the chef before the guests arrived.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — The cake had been baked by the chef before the guests arrived. (Correct):** The main clause is in Past Perfect ('had baked'), which changes to 'had been baked', while the time clause 'before the guests arrived' remains unchanged.
- **B — The cake was baked by the chef before the guests arrived.:** Using 'was baked' reduces the Past Perfect main clause to Simple Past passive.
- **C — The cake has been baked by the chef before the guests arrived.:** Using 'has been baked' shifts Past Perfect to Present Perfect passive.
- **D — The cake had been baking by the chef before the guests arrived.:** Using 'had been baking' turns Past Perfect passive into active Past Perfect Continuous.
- **Exam Extra:** In two-clause sentences, only transform the transitive clause containing the target action; leave intransitive time clauses ('before the guests arrived') untouched.

### Q537 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct active form of: "All the tickets had been sold by the booking clerk before noon."

A. The booking clerk sold all the tickets before noon.
B. The booking clerk had sold all the tickets before noon.
C. The booking clerk has sold all the tickets before noon.
D. The booking clerk was selling all the tickets before noon.

**Answer:** B. The booking clerk had sold all the tickets before noon.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The booking clerk had sold all the tickets before noon. (Correct):** 'had been sold' is Past Perfect passive, so dropping 'been' restores the Past Perfect active verb 'had sold' with 'The booking clerk' as subject.
- **A — The booking clerk sold all the tickets before noon.:** Using 'sold' shifts Past Perfect passive to Simple Past active.
- **C — The booking clerk has sold all the tickets before noon.:** Using 'has sold' shifts Past Perfect passive to Present Perfect active.
- **D — The booking clerk was selling all the tickets before noon.:** Using 'was selling' shifts Past Perfect passive to Past Continuous active.
- **Exam Extra:** Double-check irregular V3 forms in active/passive conversions, such as 'sell -> sold -> sold' and 'write -> wrote -> written'.

### Q538 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "They will have submitted the assignments by Friday."

A. The assignments will be submitted by them by Friday.
B. The assignments would have been submitted by them by Friday.
C. The assignments will have submitted by them by Friday.
D. The assignments will have been submitted by them by Friday.

**Answer:** D. The assignments will have been submitted by them by Friday.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The assignments will have been submitted by them by Friday. (Correct):** Future Perfect active ('will have submitted') becomes 'will/shall + have been + V3', giving 'will have been submitted'.
- **A — The assignments will be submitted by them by Friday.:** Using 'will be submitted' shifts Future Perfect to Simple Future passive.
- **B — The assignments would have been submitted by them by Friday.:** Using 'would have been submitted' replaces 'will' with 'would'.
- **C — The assignments will have submitted by them by Friday.:** Using 'will have submitted' omits 'been', leaving the verb in active Future Perfect.
- **Exam Extra:** Future Perfect Passive formula: Object + will/shall + have been + V3 + by + Subject. Note that 'have' never changes to 'has' after 'will'.

### Q539 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct passive form of: "The engineers will have constructed the tunnel by next year."

A. The tunnel will be constructed by the engineers by next year.
B. The tunnel has been constructed by the engineers by next year.
C. The tunnel will have been constructed by the engineers by next year.
D. The tunnel had been constructed by the engineers by next year.

**Answer:** C. The tunnel will have been constructed by the engineers by next year.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The tunnel will have been constructed by the engineers by next year. (Correct):** Future Perfect active ('will have constructed') changes to 'will have been constructed' when 'The tunnel' becomes the passive subject.
- **A — The tunnel will be constructed by the engineers by next year.:** Using 'will be constructed' shifts Future Perfect to Simple Future passive.
- **B — The tunnel has been constructed by the engineers by next year.:** Using 'has been constructed' shifts Future Perfect to Present Perfect passive.
- **D — The tunnel had been constructed by the engineers by next year.:** Using 'had been constructed' shifts Future Perfect to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Time expressions like 'by next year' or 'by Friday' signal Future Perfect when paired with 'will have + V3' and remain at the end of the passive sentence.

### Q540 · Perfect Tenses

Select the correct active form of: "The manuscript will have been edited by the proofreader by evening."

A. The proofreader will have edited the manuscript by evening.
B. The proofreader will edit the manuscript by evening.
C. The proofreader has edited the manuscript by evening.
D. The proofreader would have edited the manuscript by evening.

**Answer:** A. The proofreader will have edited the manuscript by evening.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — The proofreader will have edited the manuscript by evening. (Correct):** 'will have been edited' is Future Perfect passive; removing 'been' gives Future Perfect active 'will have edited' with 'The proofreader' as subject.
- **B — The proofreader will edit the manuscript by evening.:** Using 'will edit' simplifies Future Perfect passive to Simple Future active.
- **C — The proofreader has edited the manuscript by evening.:** Using 'has edited' shifts Future Perfect passive to Present Perfect active.
- **D — The proofreader would have edited the manuscript by evening.:** Using 'would have edited' replaces 'will' with conditional 'would'.
- **Exam Extra:** Future Perfect is the only future aspect with 'have been + V3' in passive voice.

### Q541 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct passive form of: "Even a child can solve this puzzle."

A. This puzzle could be solved even by a child.
B. This puzzle can be solved even by a child.
C. This puzzle can solved even by a child.
D. This puzzle is solved even by a child.

**Answer:** B. This puzzle can be solved even by a child.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — This puzzle can be solved even by a child. (Correct):** Present modal active ('can solve') changes to 'can + be + V3' in passive voice, keeping the adverb 'even' before 'by a child'.
- **A — This puzzle could be solved even by a child.:** Using 'could be solved' improperly changes the present modal 'can' to 'could'.
- **C — This puzzle can solved even by a child.:** Using 'can solved' omits the mandatory base verb 'be' between 'can' and the past participle.
- **D — This puzzle is solved even by a child.:** Using 'is solved' drops the modal 'can', altering the meaning of ability/possibility.
- **Exam Extra:** Modal Passive formula: Object + modal (can/could/may/might/must/should) + be + V3 + by + Subject. Never alter the modal itself.

### Q542 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct passive form of: "Passengers must wear seatbelts during takeoff."

A. Seatbelts should be worn by passengers during takeoff.
B. Seatbelts must have been worn by passengers during takeoff.
C. Seatbelts must be worn by passengers during takeoff.
D. Seatbelts are worn by passengers during takeoff.

**Answer:** C. Seatbelts must be worn by passengers during takeoff.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Seatbelts must be worn by passengers during takeoff. (Correct):** Modal 'must + V1' converts directly to 'must + be + V3', preserving the strong obligation expressed by 'must'.
- **A — Seatbelts should be worn by passengers during takeoff.:** Using 'should be worn' weakens the mandatory obligation 'must' to mild advice 'should'.
- **B — Seatbelts must have been worn by passengers during takeoff.:** Using 'must have been worn' shifts a present/general rule into a past deduction.
- **D — Seatbelts are worn by passengers during takeoff.:** Using 'are worn' removes the modal 'must', turning an obligation into a plain factual statement.
- **Exam Extra:** Keep the exact modal auxiliary from the active sentence; replacing 'must' with 'should' or 'may' changes the degree of obligation or certainty.

### Q543 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct active form of: "Traffic rules should be obeyed by every citizen."

A. Every citizen must obey traffic rules.
B. Every citizen obeys traffic rules.
C. Every citizen should have obeyed traffic rules.
D. Every citizen should obey traffic rules.

**Answer:** D. Every citizen should obey traffic rules.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Every citizen should obey traffic rules. (Correct):** 'should be obeyed' is modal passive, so dropping 'be' and restoring V1 gives 'should obey' with 'Every citizen' as the active subject.
- **A — Every citizen must obey traffic rules.:** Using 'must obey' replaces the original modal 'should' with 'must'.
- **B — Every citizen obeys traffic rules.:** Using 'obeys' omits the modal 'should', changing advice into a Simple Present fact.
- **C — Every citizen should have obeyed traffic rules.:** Using 'should have obeyed' turns 'should be + V3' into a past unfulfilled obligation.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember to preserve the exact modal ('should') and change V3 ('obeyed') back to bare V1 ('obey') when converting modal passives to active voice.

### Q544 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct passive form of: "The principal may cancel tomorrow's assembly."

A. Tomorrow's assembly may be cancelled by the principal.
B. Tomorrow's assembly might be cancelled by the principal.
C. Tomorrow's assembly may have been cancelled by the principal.
D. Tomorrow's assembly will be cancelled by the principal.

**Answer:** A. Tomorrow's assembly may be cancelled by the principal.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Tomorrow's assembly may be cancelled by the principal. (Correct):** Modal 'may + V1' ('may cancel') becomes 'may + be + V3' ('may be cancelled') in the passive voice.
- **B — Tomorrow's assembly might be cancelled by the principal.:** Using 'might be cancelled' alters the modal 'may' to 'might'.
- **C — Tomorrow's assembly may have been cancelled by the principal.:** Using 'may have been cancelled' shifts present/future possibility ('may + V1') to past possibility ('may have + V3').
- **D — Tomorrow's assembly will be cancelled by the principal.:** Using 'will be cancelled' replaces uncertain 'may' with certain 'will'.
- **Exam Extra:** Possibility modals ('may', 'might') must remain unchanged in voice transformations; do not swap 'may' for 'might' or 'will'.

### Q545 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct passive form of: "Nobody could lift the heavy iron chest."

A. The heavy iron chest cannot be lifted by anyone.
B. The heavy iron chest could not be lifted by anyone.
C. The heavy iron chest could not have been lifted by anyone.
D. The heavy iron chest was not lifted by nobody.

**Answer:** B. The heavy iron chest could not be lifted by anyone.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The heavy iron chest could not be lifted by anyone. (Correct):** Negative indefinite subject 'Nobody' transforms into 'not ... by anyone' in passive voice, and 'could + V1' becomes 'could not be lifted'.
- **A — The heavy iron chest cannot be lifted by anyone.:** Using 'cannot be lifted' shifts the past modal 'could' to present 'can'.
- **C — The heavy iron chest could not have been lifted by anyone.:** Using 'could not have been lifted' converts 'could + V1' into the past perfect modal form.
- **D — The heavy iron chest was not lifted by nobody.:** Using 'was not lifted by nobody' drops 'could' and creates an ungrammatical double negative ('not ... nobody').
- **Exam Extra:** In passive voice, 'Nobody + V' becomes 'not + V3 + by anyone' (and 'Nothing' becomes 'not ... by anything') to avoid double negatives.

### Q546 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct passive form of: "You should have informed the manager earlier."

A. The manager should be informed by you earlier.
B. The manager had been informed by you earlier.
C. The manager should have been informed by you earlier.
D. The manager would have been informed by you earlier.

**Answer:** C. The manager should have been informed by you earlier.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The manager should have been informed by you earlier. (Correct):** Past modal 'should have + V3' converts to 'should have been + V3' in passive voice, giving 'should have been informed'.
- **A — The manager should be informed by you earlier.:** Using 'should be informed' omits 'have been', changing a past action ('should have + V3') to present/future advice.
- **B — The manager had been informed by you earlier.:** Using 'had been informed' drops the modal 'should' and turns the clause into Past Perfect passive.
- **D — The manager would have been informed by you earlier.:** Using 'would have been informed' replaces the modal 'should' with 'would'.
- **Exam Extra:** Past Modal Passive formula: Object + modal + have been + V3 + by + Subject. Never drop 'have been' in past modal passives.

### Q547 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct active form of: "The confidential file must have been misplaced by the assistant."

A. The assistant must misplace the confidential file.
B. The assistant might have misplaced the confidential file.
C. The assistant had misplaced the confidential file.
D. The assistant must have misplaced the confidential file.

**Answer:** D. The assistant must have misplaced the confidential file.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The assistant must have misplaced the confidential file. (Correct):** 'must have been misplaced' is a past modal passive; removing 'been' yields the active form 'must have misplaced'.
- **A — The assistant must misplace the confidential file.:** Using 'must misplace' converts 'must have been + V3' to a present/future modal instead of a past modal.
- **B — The assistant might have misplaced the confidential file.:** Using 'might have misplaced' weakens the strong certainty of 'must' to 'might'.
- **C — The assistant had misplaced the confidential file.:** Using 'had misplaced' omits the modal 'must', shifting the sentence to Past Perfect active.
- **Exam Extra:** 'must have + V3' expresses strong logical deduction about a past event, and its passive always keeps 'have been + V3'.

### Q548 · Modals and Semi-Modals

Select the correct passive form of: "The auditor had to verify every receipt."

A. Every receipt had to be verified by the auditor.
B. Every receipt has to be verified by the auditor.
C. Every receipt had been verified by the auditor.
D. Every receipt was to be verified by the auditor.

**Answer:** A. Every receipt had to be verified by the auditor.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Every receipt had to be verified by the auditor. (Correct):** Semi-modal 'had to + V1' changes to 'had to + be + V3' in passive voice, giving 'had to be verified'.
- **B — Every receipt has to be verified by the auditor.:** Using 'has to be verified' shifts past obligation 'had to' to present obligation 'has to'.
- **C — Every receipt had been verified by the auditor.:** Using 'had been verified' drops 'to', turning the semi-modal into Past Perfect passive.
- **D — Every receipt was to be verified by the auditor.:** Using 'was to be verified' replaces compulsory 'had to' with scheduled 'was to'.
- **Exam Extra:** 'has/have/had + to + V1' converts to 'has/have/had + to be + V3', with 'has/have' adjusting to the number of the new passive subject.

### Q549 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Open the window."

A. Let the window be opened.
B. Let the window is opened.
C. The window should be opened.
D. Let the window open.

**Answer:** A. Let the window be opened.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Let the window be opened. (Correct):** A direct transitive imperative ('V1 + Object') takes the standard passive structure 'Let + Object + be + V3': "Let the window be opened."
- **B — Let the window is opened.:** Using 'is opened' wrongly inserts the finite verb 'is' instead of the bare infinitive 'be' after 'Let'.
- **C — The window should be opened.:** Using 'should be opened' turns a direct command into mild advice instead of the standard 'Let' passive form.
- **D — Let the window open.:** Using 'Let the window open' omits 'be' and leaves 'open' in active form.
- **Exam Extra:** Imperative Command Passive formula: Let + Object + be + V3. Always use bare 'be' after 'Let + Object', never 'is/are' or 'to be'.

### Q550 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Do not pluck the flowers."

A. Let the flowers not to be plucked.
B. Let the flowers not be plucked.
C. Let not the flowers are plucked.
D. The flowers were not plucked.

**Answer:** B. Let the flowers not be plucked.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Let the flowers not be plucked. (Correct):** A negative transitive imperative ('Do not + V1 + Object') becomes 'Let + Object + not + be + V3': "Let the flowers not be plucked."
- **A — Let the flowers not to be plucked.:** Using 'not to be plucked' incorrectly inserts 'to' after 'Let', which takes a bare infinitive.
- **C — Let not the flowers are plucked.:** Using 'are plucked' employs a finite verb 'are' instead of bare 'be' after 'Let'.
- **D — The flowers were not plucked.:** Using 'were not plucked' converts a negative command into a Simple Past assertive statement.
- **Exam Extra:** In negative 'Let' passives, place 'not' between the object and 'be' ('Let + Object + not + be + V3') and never insert 'to' before 'be'.

### Q551 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Please submit your application before 5 p.m."

A. Let your application be submitted before 5 p.m.
B. You are ordered to submit your application before 5 p.m.
C. You are requested to submit your application before 5 p.m.
D. You were requested to submit your application before 5 p.m.

**Answer:** C. You are requested to submit your application before 5 p.m.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — You are requested to submit your application before 5 p.m. (Correct):** Imperatives beginning with 'Please' or 'Kindly' drop the politeness word and take 'You are requested to + V1...' in passive voice.
- **A — Let your application be submitted before 5 p.m.:** Using 'Let your application be submitted' drops the polite request sense of 'Please'.
- **B — You are ordered to submit your application before 5 p.m.:** Using 'You are ordered to' misrepresents a polite request ('Please') as a stern command.
- **D — You were requested to submit your application before 5 p.m.:** Using 'You were requested to' shifts the present imperative into Simple Past.
- **Exam Extra:** Do not keep 'please' or 'kindly' after writing 'You are requested to...'; the verb 'requested' already conveys the politeness.

### Q552 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Help the needy."

A. Let the needy are helped.
B. The needy were helped.
C. You are ordered to help the needy.
D. The needy should be helped.

**Answer:** D. The needy should be helped.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The needy should be helped. (Correct):** Imperatives expressing moral duty or general advice with an object take 'Object + should be + V3': "The needy should be helped."
- **A — Let the needy are helped.:** Using 'Let the needy are helped' uses finite 'are' instead of bare 'be' after 'Let'.
- **B — The needy were helped.:** Using 'The needy were helped' turns an imperative advice into a Simple Past statement.
- **C — You are ordered to help the needy.:** Using 'You are ordered to' misclassifies moral advice as an authoritative order.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC exams distinguish direct commands ('Open the door -> Let the door be opened') from moral/universal duties ('Love your country -> Your country should be loved').

### Q553 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Walk carefully on the wet floor."

A. Let the wet floor be walked carefully.
B. You are advised to walk carefully on the wet floor.
C. You are advised to walking carefully on the wet floor.
D. You were advised to walk carefully on the wet floor.

**Answer:** B. You are advised to walk carefully on the wet floor.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — You are advised to walk carefully on the wet floor. (Correct):** Since 'Walk' is an intransitive verb with no direct object, the imperative converts using 'You are advised to + V1...' based on its advisory tone.
- **A — Let the wet floor be walked carefully.:** Using 'Let the wet floor be walked' treats the prepositional object 'wet floor' as a direct object and drops 'on'.
- **C — You are advised to walking carefully on the wet floor.:** Using 'to walking' wrongly places a gerund after 'to' instead of the base verb 'walk'.
- **D — You were advised to walk carefully on the wet floor.:** Using 'You were advised to' shifts a present imperative into Simple Past.
- **Exam Extra:** Intransitive imperatives cannot form 'Let + Object + be + V3'; they must begin with 'You are ordered/advised/requested to + V1'.

### Q554 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct active form of: "Let the lights be switched off before leaving."

A. Switch off the lights before leaving.
B. You switched off the lights before leaving.
C. Do not switch off the lights before leaving.
D. Switching off the lights before leaving.

**Answer:** A. Switch off the lights before leaving.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Switch off the lights before leaving. (Correct):** 'Let the lights be switched off before leaving' follows 'Let + Object + be + V3', whose direct active form is the imperative "Switch off the lights before leaving."
- **B — You switched off the lights before leaving.:** Using 'You switched off' turns the imperative into a Simple Past declarative sentence.
- **C — Do not switch off the lights before leaving.:** Using 'Do not switch off' introduces a negation absent from the passive sentence.
- **D — Switching off the lights before leaving.:** Using 'Switching off' creates a participial fragment with no finite imperative verb.
- **Exam Extra:** To reverse a 'Let + Object + be + V3' passive to active voice, drop 'Let' and 'be', and begin with the V1 base verb followed by the object.

### Q555 · Imperative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Let us wait for the results."

A. Let the results be waited by us.
B. You are ordered to wait for the results.
C. It is suggested that we should wait for the results.
D. It was suggested that we should wait for the results.

**Answer:** C. It is suggested that we should wait for the results.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — It is suggested that we should wait for the results. (Correct):** A collective suggestion starting with 'Let us + V1' (with an intransitive verb) transforms into 'It is suggested that we should + V1...'.
- **A — Let the results be waited by us.:** Using 'Let the results be waited by us' drops the mandatory preposition 'for' and forces a 'Let + O' construction on an intransitive suggestion.
- **B — You are ordered to wait for the results.:** Using 'You are ordered to' misinterprets a cooperative suggestion ('Let us') as an order to 'you'.
- **D — It was suggested that we should wait for the results.:** Using 'It was suggested' shifts the present suggestion into Simple Past.
- **Exam Extra:** 'Let us + intransitive V1' converts to 'It is suggested that we should + V1'; never convert a 'Let us' suggestion into 'You are ordered'.

### Q556 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Does the shopkeeper sell fresh vegetables?"

A. Is fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper?
B. Were fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper?
C. Are fresh vegetables being sold by the shopkeeper?
D. Are fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper?

**Answer:** D. Are fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Are fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper? (Correct):** Simple Present interrogatives ('Do/Does + S + V1 + O?') convert to 'Is/Am/Are + O + V3 + by + S?'. Since 'fresh vegetables' is plural, 'Are fresh vegetables sold...' is correct.
- **A — Is fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper?:** Using singular 'Is' creates a subject-verb agreement error with plural 'fresh vegetables'.
- **B — Were fresh vegetables sold by the shopkeeper?:** Using 'Were' shifts Simple Present interrogative to Simple Past passive.
- **C — Are fresh vegetables being sold by the shopkeeper?:** Using 'being sold' inserts 'being', changing Simple Present to Present Continuous passive.
- **Exam Extra:** An interrogative active sentence must remain interrogative in passive voice, with the passive auxiliary placed before the new subject.

### Q557 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Did Rohan complete the assignment on time?"

A. Was the assignment completed by Rohan on time?
B. Did the assignment completed by Rohan on time?
C. Is the assignment completed by Rohan on time?
D. Had the assignment been completed by Rohan on time?

**Answer:** A. Was the assignment completed by Rohan on time?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Was the assignment completed by Rohan on time? (Correct):** Simple Past interrogative ('Did + S + V1 + O?') converts to 'Was/Were + O + V3 + by + S?'. With singular 'the assignment', 'Was the assignment completed...' is right.
- **B — Did the assignment completed by Rohan on time?:** Using 'Did the assignment completed' keeps the active auxiliary 'Did' instead of replacing it with 'Was'.
- **C — Is the assignment completed by Rohan on time?:** Using 'Is the assignment completed' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **D — Had the assignment been completed by Rohan on time?:** Using 'Had the assignment been completed' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Active 'Did + S + V1' always becomes 'Was/Were + Object + V3' in passive questions; 'Did' is never retained.

### Q558 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Who broke the antique vase?"

A. By whom the antique vase was broken?
B. By whom was the antique vase broken?
C. Who was the antique vase broken?
D. By whom had the antique vase been broken?

**Answer:** B. By whom was the antique vase broken?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — By whom was the antique vase broken? (Correct):** 'Who' in the active subject position becomes 'By whom' at the start of the passive question, followed immediately by the auxiliary 'was' before the subject 'the antique vase'.
- **A — By whom the antique vase was broken?:** Placing 'the antique vase' before 'was' ('By whom the antique vase was') breaks interrogative subject-auxiliary inversion.
- **C — Who was the antique vase broken?:** Using 'Who' instead of 'By whom' omits the agentive preposition 'By' and uses the wrong pronoun case.
- **D — By whom had the antique vase been broken?:** Using 'had ... been broken' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Watch word order in 'By whom' questions: 'By whom + auxiliary + subject + V3?' (never 'By whom + subject + auxiliary').

### Q559 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Who is teaching you French this semester?"

A. By whom are you taught French this semester?
B. By whom you are being taught French this semester?
C. By whom are you being taught French this semester?
D. By whom were you being taught French this semester?

**Answer:** C. By whom are you being taught French this semester?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — By whom are you being taught French this semester? (Correct):** Present Continuous 'Who is teaching you...' changes to 'By whom + are + you + being + taught...?', placing 'are' before 'you' and 'being' before V3.
- **A — By whom are you taught French this semester?:** Using 'are you taught' omits 'being', reducing Present Continuous to Simple Present passive.
- **B — By whom you are being taught French this semester?:** Using 'By whom you are being taught' fails to invert 'are' before 'you' in a question.
- **D — By whom were you being taught French this semester?:** Using 'were you being taught' shifts Present Continuous to Past Continuous passive.
- **Exam Extra:** In multi-word passive verbs ('are being taught', 'has been done'), only the first auxiliary moves before the subject in questions.

### Q560 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct active form of: "By whom was this inspiring speech written?"

A. Who has written this inspiring speech?
B. Whom wrote this inspiring speech?
C. Who had written this inspiring speech?
D. Who wrote this inspiring speech?

**Answer:** D. Who wrote this inspiring speech?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Who wrote this inspiring speech? (Correct):** 'By whom was ... written?' is Simple Past passive, so its active form restores 'By whom' to 'Who' and uses Simple Past active 'wrote'.
- **A — Who has written this inspiring speech?:** Using 'has written' shifts Simple Past passive to Present Perfect active.
- **B — Whom wrote this inspiring speech?:** Using 'Whom' puts the object pronoun in the subject slot instead of 'Who'.
- **C — Who had written this inspiring speech?:** Using 'had written' shifts Simple Past passive to Past Perfect active.
- **Exam Extra:** In active questions starting with 'Who' as the subject, no 'do/did' auxiliary is used in affirmative Simple Present or Simple Past ('Who wrote this?').

### Q561 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Whom did the committee nominate for the award?"

A. Who was nominated by the committee for the award?
B. Whom was nominated by the committee for the award?
C. Who is nominated by the committee for the award?
D. Who had been nominated by the committee for the award?

**Answer:** A. Who was nominated by the committee for the award?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Who was nominated by the committee for the award? (Correct):** Object pronoun 'Whom' in active voice becomes the subject pronoun 'Who' in passive voice, and Simple Past 'did ... nominate' becomes 'was nominated'.
- **B — Whom was nominated by the committee for the award?:** Using 'Whom' keeps the objective pronoun in the passive subject position instead of 'Who'.
- **C — Who is nominated by the committee for the award?:** Using 'is nominated' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **D — Who had been nominated by the committee for the award?:** Using 'had been nominated' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember the pair: Active 'Who' -> Passive 'By whom'; Active 'Whom' -> Passive 'Who'.

### Q562 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Have the officials inspected the warehouse?"

A. Have the warehouse been inspected by the officials?
B. Has the warehouse been inspected by the officials?
C. Was the warehouse inspected by the officials?
D. Has the warehouse inspected by the officials?

**Answer:** B. Has the warehouse been inspected by the officials?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Has the warehouse been inspected by the officials? (Correct):** Present Perfect interrogative ('Have + S + V3 + O?') changes to 'Has/Have + O + been + V3 + by + S?'. Singular 'the warehouse' requires 'Has'.
- **A — Have the warehouse been inspected by the officials?:** Using 'Have' creates a subject-verb agreement error with the singular subject 'the warehouse'.
- **C — Was the warehouse inspected by the officials?:** Using 'Was ... inspected' shifts Present Perfect to Simple Past passive.
- **D — Has the warehouse inspected by the officials?:** Using 'Has ... inspected' omits 'been', leaving the sentence in active voice.
- **Exam Extra:** Check whether the new passive subject changes plural 'Have' to singular 'Has' (or vice versa) at the very start of Present Perfect questions.

### Q563 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "Why should we suspect an innocent person?"

A. Why should an innocent person suspected by us?
B. Why an innocent person should be suspected by us?
C. Why should an innocent person be suspected by us?
D. Why would an innocent person be suspected by us?

**Answer:** C. Why should an innocent person be suspected by us?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Why should an innocent person be suspected by us? (Correct):** Wh-questions with modals follow 'Wh-word + modal + Object + be + V3 + by + Subject?': "Why should an innocent person be suspected by us?"
- **A — Why should an innocent person suspected by us?:** Using 'suspected' omits the mandatory 'be' between the subject and V3 in a modal passive.
- **B — Why an innocent person should be suspected by us?:** Using 'Why an innocent person should' fails to invert 'should' before the subject 'an innocent person'.
- **D — Why would an innocent person be suspected by us?:** Using 'would' replaces the original modal 'should'.
- **Exam Extra:** Wh-words ('Why', 'When', 'Where', 'How') stay fixed at the front of the question; inversion happens right after them with the first auxiliary.

### Q564 · Interrogative Sentences

Select the correct passive form of: "What do you expect from this training?"

A. What is expected by you from this training?
B. What was expected by you from this training?
C. What is expecting by you from this training?
D. What are expected by you from this training?

**Answer:** A. What is expected by you from this training?
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — What is expected by you from this training? (Correct):** 'What' asks for the missing object and serves as the singular passive subject here, turning Simple Present 'do you expect' into 'is expected by you'.
- **B — What was expected by you from this training?:** Using 'was expected' shifts Simple Present to Simple Past passive.
- **C — What is expecting by you from this training?:** Using 'is expecting' uses active Present Continuous instead of passive Simple Present ('is expected').
- **D — What are expected by you from this training?:** Using 'are expected' uses a plural auxiliary with singular interrogative 'What'.
- **Exam Extra:** When 'What' acts as the subject of a passive question without a plural noun, it takes a singular verb ('is/was/has').

### Q565 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct passive form of: "The manager offered me a better position."

A. I am offered a better position by the manager.
B. I was offered a better position by the manager.
C. I had been offered a better position by the manager.
D. A better position was being offered to me by the manager.

**Answer:** B. I was offered a better position by the manager.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — I was offered a better position by the manager. (Correct):** Ditransitive verbs like 'offer' take two objects ('me' and 'a better position'). Making the indirect object 'me' the subject yields "I was offered a better position by the manager."
- **A — I am offered a better position by the manager.:** Using 'am offered' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **C — I had been offered a better position by the manager.:** Using 'had been offered' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **D — A better position was being offered to me by the manager.:** Using 'was being offered' adds 'being', shifting Simple Past to Past Continuous passive.
- **Exam Extra:** When converting a double-object sentence using the indirect object as subject, change the pronoun case ('me -> I', 'him -> he', 'them -> they').

### Q566 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct active form of: "A medal of honour was awarded to the brave firefighter by the governor."

A. The governor awards the brave firefighter a medal of honour.
B. The governor had awarded the brave firefighter a medal of honour.
C. The governor was awarding the brave firefighter a medal of honour.
D. The governor awarded the brave firefighter a medal of honour.

**Answer:** D. The governor awarded the brave firefighter a medal of honour.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The governor awarded the brave firefighter a medal of honour. (Correct):** 'was awarded' is Simple Past passive. Converting to active voice restores 'The governor' as subject and 'awarded' as Simple Past verb before the two objects.
- **A — The governor awards the brave firefighter a medal of honour.:** Using 'awards' shifts Simple Past passive to Simple Present active.
- **B — The governor had awarded the brave firefighter a medal of honour.:** Using 'had awarded' shifts Simple Past passive to Past Perfect active.
- **C — The governor was awarding the brave firefighter a medal of honour.:** Using 'was awarding' shifts Simple Past passive to Past Continuous active.
- **Exam Extra:** In active voice, the preposition 'to' or 'for' before the indirect object is dropped when the indirect object is placed right after the verb ('awarded the firefighter a medal').

### Q567 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct passive form of: "Everyone in the village knows him."

A. He is known by everyone in the village.
B. He was known to everyone in the village.
C. He is known to everyone in the village.
D. He is known with everyone in the village.

**Answer:** C. He is known to everyone in the village.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — He is known to everyone in the village. (Correct):** Stative verbs 'know' and 'oblige' take the fixed preposition 'to' instead of 'by' in the passive voice: "He is known to everyone in the village."
- **A — He is known by everyone in the village.:** Using 'known by' applies the default agent preposition 'by' instead of 'to', which 'known' requires in passive voice.
- **B — He was known to everyone in the village.:** Using 'was known' shifts Simple Present to Simple Past passive.
- **D — He is known with everyone in the village.:** Using 'known with' pairs 'known' with the wrong preposition 'with' instead of 'to'.
- **Exam Extra:** Key SSC fixed-preposition passives: known TO, obliged TO, surprised/astonished/amazed AT, pleased/satisfied/decorated WITH, contained/interested IN.

### Q568 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct passive form of: "Her sudden resignation surprised all her colleagues."

A. All her colleagues were surprised at her sudden resignation.
B. All her colleagues were surprised with her sudden resignation.
C. All her colleagues are surprised at her sudden resignation.
D. All her colleagues had been surprised at her sudden resignation.

**Answer:** A. All her colleagues were surprised at her sudden resignation.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — All her colleagues were surprised at her sudden resignation. (Correct):** Verbs expressing surprise or shock ('surprised', 'astonished', 'amazed', 'alarmed') take 'at' rather than 'by' in SSC standard passive usage, with Simple Past 'were surprised'.
- **B — All her colleagues were surprised with her sudden resignation.:** Using 'surprised with' uses the wrong preposition 'with'; 'surprised' takes 'at' for events or actions.
- **C — All her colleagues are surprised at her sudden resignation.:** Using 'are surprised' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **D — All her colleagues had been surprised at her sudden resignation.:** Using 'had been surprised' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Remember: 'surprised AT', 'astonished AT', 'vexed AT (something)', 'annoyed AT (something)' / 'annoyed WITH (someone)'.

### Q569 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct passive form of: "This leather briefcase contains important legal documents."

A. Important legal documents are contained by this leather briefcase.
B. Important legal documents were contained in this leather briefcase.
C. Important legal documents is contained in this leather briefcase.
D. Important legal documents are contained in this leather briefcase.

**Answer:** D. Important legal documents are contained in this leather briefcase.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Important legal documents are contained in this leather briefcase. (Correct):** The verb 'contain' takes the preposition 'in' instead of 'by' in passive voice, and plural 'Important legal documents' takes 'are contained'.
- **A — Important legal documents are contained by this leather briefcase.:** Using 'contained by' uses the wrong preposition 'by' instead of 'in'.
- **B — Important legal documents were contained in this leather briefcase.:** Using 'were contained' shifts Simple Present to Simple Past passive.
- **C — Important legal documents is contained in this leather briefcase.:** Using 'is contained' creates a number disagreement with the plural subject 'Important legal documents'.
- **Exam Extra:** Both 'contained IN' and 'interested IN' replace 'by' with 'in' in the passive voice.

### Q570 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct passive form of: "The naughty boys laughed at the old beggar."

A. The old beggar was laughed by the naughty boys.
B. The old beggar was laughed at by the naughty boys.
C. The old beggar is laughed at by the naughty boys.
D. The old beggar was being laughed at by the naughty boys.

**Answer:** B. The old beggar was laughed at by the naughty boys.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The old beggar was laughed at by the naughty boys. (Correct):** Phrasal/prepositional verbs like 'laugh at' must retain their preposition 'at' immediately after V3 before adding 'by': "was laughed at by".
- **A — The old beggar was laughed by the naughty boys.:** Using 'was laughed by' omits the mandatory preposition 'at' belonging to the phrasal verb 'laugh at'.
- **C — The old beggar is laughed at by the naughty boys.:** Using 'is laughed at' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **D — The old beggar was being laughed at by the naughty boys.:** Using 'was being laughed at' adds 'being', changing Simple Past to Past Continuous passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Never drop the preposition of a phrasal verb ('laugh at', 'look after', 'look into', 'stare at') even when 'by' immediately follows it.

### Q571 · Double Objects, Fixed Prepositions and Causatives

Select the correct passive form of: "The coach made the players run five laps."

A. The players were made run five laps by the coach.
B. The players are made to run five laps by the coach.
C. The players were made to run five laps by the coach.
D. The players had been made to run five laps by the coach.

**Answer:** C. The players were made to run five laps by the coach.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The players were made to run five laps by the coach. (Correct):** Causative/perception verbs ('make', 'see', 'hear') take a bare infinitive in active voice ('made ... run') but require a full 'to-infinitive' in passive voice ('were made to run').
- **A — The players were made run five laps by the coach.:** Using 'were made run' retains the bare infinitive 'run' instead of adding 'to' in the passive voice.
- **B — The players are made to run five laps by the coach.:** Using 'are made to run' shifts Simple Past to Simple Present passive.
- **D — The players had been made to run five laps by the coach.:** Using 'had been made to run' shifts Simple Past to Past Perfect passive.
- **Exam Extra:** Active 'make + Object + V1' always becomes Passive 'be made + to + V1'; only 'let' keeps a bare infinitive in passive.

### Q572 · Infinitives and Quasi-Passive Verbs

Select the correct passive form of: "It is time to close the shop."

A. It is time for the shop to be closed.
B. It is time the shop to be closed.
C. It is time for the shop to close.
D. It was time for the shop to be closed.

**Answer:** A. It is time for the shop to be closed.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — It is time for the shop to be closed. (Correct):** The pattern 'It is time + to + V1 + Object' converts to 'It is time + for + Object + to be + V3': "It is time for the shop to be closed."
- **B — It is time the shop to be closed.:** Omitting 'for' before 'the shop' breaks the required 'It is time + for + Object + to be + V3' pattern.
- **C — It is time for the shop to close.:** Using 'to close' leaves the infinitive in active form instead of passive 'to be closed'.
- **D — It was time for the shop to be closed.:** Using 'It was time' changes the present introductory clause 'It is time' to Simple Past.
- **Exam Extra:** In 'It is time to...' transformations, always insert 'for' before the object and change 'to + V1' to 'to be + V3'.

### Q573 · Infinitives and Quasi-Passive Verbs

Select the correct passive form of: "The secretary is to type three letters today."

A. Three letters are to type by the secretary today.
B. Three letters is to be typed by the secretary today.
C. Three letters were to be typed by the secretary today.
D. Three letters are to be typed by the secretary today.

**Answer:** D. Three letters are to be typed by the secretary today.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Three letters are to be typed by the secretary today. (Correct):** 'is/am/are + to + V1' becomes 'is/am/are + to be + V3' in passive voice. Since 'Three letters' is plural, 'are to be typed' is correct.
- **A — Three letters are to type by the secretary today.:** Using 'are to type' omits 'be', leaving the infinitive in active voice.
- **B — Three letters is to be typed by the secretary today.:** Using 'is to be typed' disagrees in number with the plural subject 'Three letters'.
- **C — Three letters were to be typed by the secretary today.:** Using 'were to be typed' shifts Simple Present 'is' to Simple Past 'were'.
- **Exam Extra:** Passive of 'be + to + V1' keeps the 'be' form adjusted to the new subject followed by 'to be + V3'.

### Q574 · Infinitives and Quasi-Passive Verbs

Select the correct passive form of: "There is no time to lose."

A. There is no time for losing.
B. There is no time to be lost.
C. There was no time to be lost.
D. There is no time to be losing.

**Answer:** B. There is no time to be lost.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — There is no time to be lost. (Correct):** Introductory 'There is + Noun + to + V1' transforms into 'There is + Noun + to be + V3': "There is no time to be lost."
- **A — There is no time for losing.:** Using 'for losing' replaces the infinitive structure with a prepositional gerund.
- **C — There was no time to be lost.:** Using 'There was' shifts the present tense 'is' to Simple Past 'was'.
- **D — There is no time to be losing.:** Using 'to be losing' uses a continuous infinitive instead of the passive infinitive 'to be lost'.
- **Exam Extra:** In 'There is/are + Noun + to + V1' sentences, only the infinitive changes from 'to + V1' to 'to be + V3'; the 'There is/are' frame stays unchanged.

### Q575 · Infinitives and Quasi-Passive Verbs

Select the correct passive form of: "The director expects the staff to follow the safety guidelines."

A. The director expects the safety guidelines to follow by the staff.
B. The director expected the safety guidelines to be followed by the staff.
C. The director expects the safety guidelines to be followed by the staff.
D. The director expects the safety guidelines are followed by the staff.

**Answer:** C. The director expects the safety guidelines to be followed by the staff.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The director expects the safety guidelines to be followed by the staff. (Correct):** In 'Subject + verb + Object1 + to + V1 + Object2', keeping the main clause active and passivising the infinitive clause yields "The director expects the safety guidelines to be followed by the staff."
- **A — The director expects the safety guidelines to follow by the staff.:** Using 'to follow' keeps the infinitive active instead of converting it to passive 'to be followed'.
- **B — The director expected the safety guidelines to be followed by the staff.:** Using 'expected' shifts the Simple Present main verb 'expects' into Simple Past.
- **D — The director expects the safety guidelines are followed by the staff.:** Using 'are followed' replaces the passive infinitive 'to be followed' with a finite verb phrase without 'that'.
- **Exam Extra:** When 'want/like/expect/wish + A + to + V1 + B' is passivised at the infinitive, move B after the main verb and use 'to be + V3 + by + A'.

---

## Chapter 8: Direct & Indirect Speech (Narration)

### Q576 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: Rohan said to me, "I am preparing the audit report now."

A. Rohan told me that he was preparing the audit report now.
B. Rohan told me that he am preparing the audit report then.
C. Rohan told me that he was preparing the audit report then.
D. Rohan told to me that he was preparing the audit report then.

**Answer:** C. Rohan told me that he was preparing the audit report then.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Rohan told me that he was preparing the audit report then. (Correct):** 'Said to me' changes to 'told me', the conjunction 'that' is added, 'I' changes to 'he' (subject of reporting verb), present continuous 'am preparing' backshifts to past continuous 'was preparing', and 'now' changes to 'then'.
- **A — Rohan told me that he was preparing the audit report now.:** Retains 'now' instead of converting the time marker to 'then'.
- **B — Rohan told me that he am preparing the audit report then.:** Keeps the present continuous 'am preparing' without backshifting it to past continuous.
- **D — Rohan told to me that he was preparing the audit report then.:** Uses the ungrammatical construction 'told to me', as 'told' is transitive and does not take 'to'.
- **Exam Extra:** In assertive sentences, 'said to + object' becomes 'told + object' without 'to', and 'now' shifts to 'then'.

### Q577 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: Arjun said to her, "My sister writes poetry in Hindi."

A. Arjun told her that his sister wrote poetry in Hindi.
B. Arjun told her that my sister wrote poetry in Hindi.
C. Arjun told her that his sister had written poetry in Hindi.
D. Arjun told her that his sister writes poetry in Hindi.

**Answer:** A. Arjun told her that his sister wrote poetry in Hindi.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Arjun told her that his sister wrote poetry in Hindi. (Correct):** 'Said to her' changes to 'told her', 'my' changes to 'his' according to the subject 'Arjun', and the simple present verb 'writes' backshifts to the simple past 'wrote'.
- **B — Arjun told her that my sister wrote poetry in Hindi.:** Fails to change the first-person possessive 'my' to 'his' to match the male speaker Arjun.
- **C — Arjun told her that his sister had written poetry in Hindi.:** Incorrectly shifts simple present 'writes' to past perfect 'had written' instead of simple past 'wrote'.
- **D — Arjun told her that his sister writes poetry in Hindi.:** Leaves the verb 'writes' in the simple present tense despite the past reporting verb 'said'.
- **Exam Extra:** Simple present (V1/V5) in direct speech always backshifts to simple past (V2) when the reporting verb is in the past tense (except for universal truths or habits).

### Q578 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: The engineers said, "We have completed the bridge inspection."

A. The engineers told that they had completed the bridge inspection.
B. The engineers said that they have completed the bridge inspection.
C. The engineers said that we had completed the bridge inspection.
D. The engineers said that they had completed the bridge inspection.

**Answer:** D. The engineers said that they had completed the bridge inspection.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — The engineers said that they had completed the bridge inspection. (Correct):** 'Said' without an object remains 'said', 'we' changes to 'they', and present perfect 'have completed' backshifts to past perfect 'had completed'.
- **A — The engineers told that they had completed the bridge inspection.:** Wrongly converts 'said' into 'told' even though there is no object in the reporting clause.
- **B — The engineers said that they have completed the bridge inspection.:** Leaves the present perfect 'have completed' unshifted instead of changing it to 'had completed'.
- **C — The engineers said that we had completed the bridge inspection.:** Fails to change the first-person pronoun 'we' to 'they' and uses 'was' incorrectly.
- **Exam Extra:** When 'said' has no listener/object after it, it is never changed to 'told'.

### Q579 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: Meera said to her mother, "I bought a new laptop yesterday."

A. Meera told her mother that she bought a new laptop the previous day.
B. Meera told her mother that she had bought a new laptop the previous day.
C. Meera told her mother that she had bought a new laptop yesterday.
D. Meera said her mother that she had buyed a new laptop the previous day.

**Answer:** B. Meera told her mother that she had bought a new laptop the previous day.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Meera told her mother that she had bought a new laptop the previous day. (Correct):** Simple past 'bought' backshifts to past perfect 'had bought', 'I' changes to 'she', and 'yesterday' converts to 'the previous day'.
- **A — Meera told her mother that she bought a new laptop the previous day.:** Keeps the verb in simple past 'bought' instead of backshifting it to past perfect 'had bought'.
- **C — Meera told her mother that she had bought a new laptop yesterday.:** Retains the direct-speech time marker 'yesterday' instead of changing it to 'the previous day'.
- **D — Meera said her mother that she had buyed a new laptop the previous day.:** Uses the non-standard past form 'buyed' and fails to change 'said to' into 'told'.
- **Exam Extra:** Simple past (V2) in direct speech changes to past perfect (had + V3) in indirect speech.

### Q580 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: Kabir said to me, "I was waiting for you outside the library."

A. Kabir told me that he had been waiting for me outside the library.
B. Kabir told me that he was waiting for me outside the library.
C. Kabir told me that he had been waiting for you outside the library.
D. Kabir told me that he has been waiting for me outside the library.

**Answer:** A. Kabir told me that he had been waiting for me outside the library.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Kabir told me that he had been waiting for me outside the library. (Correct):** Past continuous 'was waiting' backshifts to past perfect continuous 'had been waiting', 'I' changes to 'he', and 'you' (referring to 'me') changes to 'me'.
- **B — Kabir told me that he was waiting for me outside the library.:** Leaves the verb in past continuous 'was waiting' instead of shifting it to past perfect continuous.
- **C — Kabir told me that he had been waiting for you outside the library.:** Fails to convert the second-person object pronoun 'you' to 'me'.
- **D — Kabir told me that he has been waiting for me outside the library.:** Incorrectly converts past continuous to present perfect continuous 'has been waiting'.
- **Exam Extra:** Past continuous (was/were + V-ing) changes to past perfect continuous (had been + V-ing) in indirect speech.

### Q581 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: The passengers said, "The train had already left before we reached the station."

A. The passengers said that the train already left before they reached the station.
B. The passengers said that the train had already left before we reached the station.
C. The passengers said that the train had already left before they reached the station.
D. The passengers said that the train had been already left before they reached the station.

**Answer:** C. The passengers said that the train had already left before they reached the station.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — The passengers said that the train had already left before they reached the station. (Correct):** Past perfect ('had already left') does not undergo any further tense backshift in indirect speech; only the pronoun 'the train' stays unchanged and 'before we reached' remains simple past 'before they reached' or both stay consistent, with 'we' changing to 'they'.
- **A — The passengers said that the train already left before they reached the station.:** Wrongly drops 'had' and reduces the past perfect verb to simple past 'already left'.
- **B — The passengers said that the train had already left before we reached the station.:** Fails to change the first-person pronoun 'we' to 'they' to match the plural subject 'the passengers'.
- **D — The passengers said that the train had been already left before they reached the station.:** Uses the non-existent tense form 'had been already left' (passive error) instead of active 'had already left'.
- **Exam Extra:** Past perfect (had + V3) and past perfect continuous (had been + V-ing) never change tense in indirect speech.

### Q582 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: The coach says to me, "You are making steady progress in athletics."

A. The coach tells me that I was making steady progress in athletics.
B. The coach tells me that I am making steady progress in athletics.
C. The coach tells me that you are making steady progress in athletics.
D. The coach told me that I am making steady progress in athletics.

**Answer:** B. The coach tells me that I am making steady progress in athletics.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — The coach tells me that I am making steady progress in athletics. (Correct):** Because the reporting verb 'says to' is in the present tense, it becomes 'tells' and the tense of the reported speech ('are making') does NOT backshift; only 'you' changes to 'I' and 'are' adjusts to 'am'.
- **A — The coach tells me that I was making steady progress in athletics.:** Incorrectly backshifts the verb to past continuous 'was making' even though the reporting verb 'says' is in the present tense.
- **C — The coach tells me that you are making steady progress in athletics.:** Fails to change the pronoun 'you' to 'I', which is required because the listener is 'me'.
- **D — The coach told me that I am making steady progress in athletics.:** Uses the past reporting verb 'told' instead of preserving the present reporting verb 'tells'.
- **Exam Extra:** When the reporting verb is in the present or future tense (says, has said, will say), the tense of the reported verb never changes.

### Q583 · Assertive Sentences

Convert into indirect speech: Priya will say, "I have never seen such a vibrant painting."

A. Priya will say that she had never seen such a vibrant painting.
B. Priya will say that she have never seen such a vibrant painting.
C. Priya will say that I have never seen such a vibrant painting.
D. Priya will say that she has never seen such a vibrant painting.

**Answer:** D. Priya will say that she has never seen such a vibrant painting.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Priya will say that she has never seen such a vibrant painting. (Correct):** Since the reporting verb 'will say' is in the future tense, the tense inside the reported speech ('have never seen') stays in the present perfect, adjusted to 'has never seen' for third-person singular 'she', with 'such a' remaining unchanged.
- **A — Priya will say that she had never seen such a vibrant painting.:** Wrongly backshifts 'have never seen' to past perfect 'had never seen' despite the future reporting verb.
- **B — Priya will say that she have never seen such a vibrant painting.:** Uses the plural auxiliary 'have' with the singular subject 'she'.
