- **A — Selecting the cheapest broadcast channel regardless of demographic reach:** Organisers explicitly rejected relying solely on the cheapest channel because doing so would exclude households offline.
- **B — Eliminating print media whenever digital tools offer faster delivery:** They deliberately kept print media rather than abandoning it prematurely.
- **D — Prioritizing rural households over urban residents in civic campaigning:** Using both channels ensured broad inclusion across all wards rather than favouring rural households over urban ones.
- **Exam Extra:** When a passage weighs cost against reach, the winning principle usually balances efficiency with equity of access.

### Q708 · Reading Comprehension - Factual Synthesis

A municipal maintenance crew logged turnaround times across hundreds of routine household repairs to diagnose sluggish service delivery. Surprisingly, actual technical repairs took little time; most delays stemmed from technicians arriving on site only to discover missing replacement valves or seals. Once supervisors instituted mandatory pre-dispatch inventory checks, repeat service visits fell dramatically.

What misconception did the crew's time logs dispel?

A. That household repairs rarely require specialized replacement components
B. That pre-dispatch checks slow down overall daily ticket completion
C. That repeat service visits were caused mainly by customer absences
D. That the technical complexity of repairs was the primary cause of delays

**Answer:** D. That the technical complexity of repairs was the primary cause of delays
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — That the technical complexity of repairs was the primary cause of delays (Correct):** The maintenance logs revealed that actual repairs took little time and delays came mainly from missing parts, overturning the assumption that repair complexity slowed service down.
- **A — That household repairs rarely require specialized replacement components:** Replacement valves and seals were essential to completing the jobs, which is why missing them caused delays.
- **B — That pre-dispatch checks slow down overall daily ticket completion:** Pre-dispatch inventory checks sharply reduced repeat visits and sped up service rather than slowing ticket completion.
- **C — That repeat service visits were caused mainly by customer absences:** Customer absences are not mentioned; technicians arrived on site and lacked spare parts.
- **Exam Extra:** Pay attention to words like "surprisingly" or "actually"—they signal a contrast between a common assumption and the evidence uncovered.

### Q709 · Reading Comprehension - Inference

Seeking broader inclusivity, a regional museum installed raised tactile plaques beside standard printed captions in its sculpture wing. Visitors with visual impairments reported that the labels clarified spatial details without distracting sighted patrons. Before rolling out the design across every gallery, curators retained the original text and collected structured visitor feedback during a three-month pilot.

Why did curators preserve the original captions while piloting tactile plaques?

A. To maintain existing access while testing the new feature's effectiveness before scaling
B. To comply with budget rules that barred replacing printed boards for three years
C. Because tactile labels could not convey historical dates or artist biographies
D. Because sighted visitors objected to sharing display space with raised plaques

**Answer:** A. To maintain existing access while testing the new feature's effectiveness before scaling
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — To maintain existing access while testing the new feature's effectiveness before scaling (Correct):** Keeping original printed captions during the three-month pilot preserved existing accessibility while allowing curators to gather structured feedback before scaling tactile plaques museum-wide.
- **B — To comply with budget rules that barred replacing printed boards for three years:** No multi-year budget restriction on replacing printed boards is mentioned in the passage.
- **C — Because tactile labels could not convey historical dates or artist biographies:** The passage notes tactile labels clarified spatial details without distracting sighted patrons, rather than citing any inability to convey dates.
- **D — Because sighted visitors objected to sharing display space with raised plaques:** Sighted patrons were not distracted by the raised plaques, so visitor objections did not drive the decision.
- **Exam Extra:** Words like "pilot", "before rolling out", and "feedback" signal an incremental, evidence-based implementation strategy.

### Q710 · Reading Comprehension - Main Idea

A neighbourhood association planted drought-tolerant native saplings along a dusty arterial road. Volunteers selected species adapted to local monsoon patterns and organized rotational watering exclusively through the critical first summer. Rather than launching an immediate second phase, the group tracked sapling survival rates through winter to refine future planting choices.

What does the association's approach emphasize in urban greening?

A. Choosing ornamental trees that grow rapidly with year-round artificial irrigation
B. Aligning species selection with local climate and evaluating survival before expanding
C. Avoiding community watering schedules so saplings adapt naturally from day one
D. Expanding roadside plantations every season regardless of early mortality rates

**Answer:** B. Aligning species selection with local climate and evaluating survival before expanding
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Aligning species selection with local climate and evaluating survival before expanding (Correct):** Selecting drought-tolerant species suited to local monsoon patterns and tracking survival rates before starting a second phase highlights climate alignment and measured evaluation.
- **A — Choosing ornamental trees that grow rapidly with year-round artificial irrigation:** The group chose drought-tolerant native species with watering limited to the first summer, not fast-growing ornamentals needing year-round irrigation.
- **C — Avoiding community watering schedules so saplings adapt naturally from day one:** Volunteers organized rotational watering throughout the critical first summer rather than withholding water from day one.
- **D — Expanding roadside plantations every season regardless of early mortality rates:** The association explicitly paused launching a second phase until it evaluated winter survival rates.
- **Exam Extra:** In SSC passages on pilot projects, look for two linked steps: contextual fit at launch and empirical review before expansion.

### Q711 · Reading Comprehension - Tone and Attitude

Coastal mangrove forests absorb storm surges far more effectively than concrete seawalls, yet coastal developers routinely clear them for short-term resort profits. When cyclones strike, taxpayers—not the developers—shoulder the soaring bills for emergency relief and reconstruction. Treating natural buffers as expendable wasteland is not merely ecological myopia; it is fiscal recklessness disguised as progress.

What is the author's tone toward clearing mangrove forests for coastal development?

A. Detached and purely descriptive
B. Optimistic and conciliatory
C. Critical and indignant
D. Hesitant and ambivalent

**Answer:** C. Critical and indignant
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Critical and indignant (Correct):** Phrases such as "short-term resort profits", "expendable wasteland", and "fiscal recklessness disguised as progress" convey strong disapproval and moral urgency, marking a critical and indignant tone.
- **A — Detached and purely descriptive:** A detached tone avoids value judgments, whereas this passage openly condemns clearing mangroves as recklessness.
- **B — Optimistic and conciliatory:** The author offers no compromise or soothing reassurance toward coastal developers.
- **D — Hesitant and ambivalent:** The passage takes a firm, unequivocal stand rather than expressing uncertainty or mixed feelings.
- **Exam Extra:** To identify tone in RC passages, underline emotionally charged nouns and adjectives ("myopia", "recklessness", "disguised")—they reveal whether the writer is neutral or judgmental.

### Q712 · Reading Comprehension - Vocabulary in Context

Early proponents of algorithmic hiring claimed that automated screening would purge human bias from recruitment. However, because models learn from historical employment records, they often *entrench* past inequities rather than dismantling them. Unless engineers audit training data rigorously, software merely automates prejudice at scale.

In the context of the passage, what does the word "entrench" most nearly mean?

A. Expose publicly
B. Gradually weaken
C. Completely eradicate
D. Firmly establish

**Answer:** D. Firmly establish
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Firmly establish (Correct):** Contrasted with "dismantling them", "entrench" means to embed or firmly establish existing inequities so they become harder to remove.
- **A — Expose publicly:** Exposing inequities publicly would help correct them, which contradicts the passage's warning about automating prejudice.
- **B — Gradually weaken:** Gradually weakening is closer to "dismantling" than to reinforcing historical bias.
- **C — Completely eradicate:** Eradicating means wiping out completely, which is the exact opposite of what biased training data does.
- **Exam Extra:** For vocabulary-in-context questions, use surrounding contrast clues ("rather than dismantling them") to lock in the opposite meaning before inspecting the choices.

### Q713 · Reading Comprehension - Inference

When central banks raise interest rates rapidly to tame inflation, borrowing costs climb for small enterprises faster than consumer prices fall. Large corporations with deep cash reserves can weather this squeeze, whereas smaller firms often defer expansion or trim payrolls. Consequently, uniform monetary tightening can inadvertently tilt market share toward dominant incumbents.

Which inference is best supported by the passage?

A. Sharp interest-rate hikes can disproportionately burden smaller businesses compared with cash-rich firms.
B. Raising interest rates immediately eliminates inflation without affecting employment levels.
C. Large corporations rely more heavily on short-term loans than small enterprises do.
D. Central banks intentionally raise rates to drive small businesses out of competitive markets.

**Answer:** A. Sharp interest-rate hikes can disproportionately burden smaller businesses compared with cash-rich firms.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Sharp interest-rate hikes can disproportionately burden smaller businesses compared with cash-rich firms. (Correct):** The passage contrasts cash-rich large corporations, which can absorb higher borrowing costs, with smaller firms that must defer expansion or trim payrolls—supporting the inference that rate hikes hit smaller businesses harder.
- **B — Raising interest rates immediately eliminates inflation without affecting employment levels.:** The passage notes that consumer prices fall more slowly than borrowing costs rise and that smaller firms may trim payrolls.
- **C — Large corporations rely more heavily on short-term loans than small enterprises do.:** Large corporations rely on deep cash reserves rather than heavy short-term borrowing, enabling them to weather the squeeze.
- **D — Central banks intentionally raise rates to drive small businesses out of competitive markets.:** The passage states this concentration happens "inadvertently", not as an intentional goal of central banks.
- **Exam Extra:** Notice softening verbs like "can" in accurate inferences; SSC keys usually favour measured, well-supported statements over sweeping absolutes.

### Q714 · Reading Comprehension - Main Idea

Ancient maritime trade across the Indian Ocean relied less on brute naval conquest than on predictable seasonal monsoon winds. Mariners from East Africa, Arabia, and the Malabar Coast timed their voyages to catch favourable currents, fostering cosmopolitan port cities long before European caravels arrived. Shared commercial trust, rather than imperial decrees, sustained these networks across centuries.

What is the central idea of the passage?

A. European caravels introduced the first reliable long-distance navigation across the Indian Ocean.
B. Monsoon rhythms and mutual commercial trust underpinned enduring pre-colonial Indian Ocean trade.
C. Imperial naval fleets enforced trade monopolies between East Africa and the Malabar Coast.
D. Ancient mariners avoided port cities because seasonal storms made coastal trade hazardous.

**Answer:** B. Monsoon rhythms and mutual commercial trust underpinned enduring pre-colonial Indian Ocean trade.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Monsoon rhythms and mutual commercial trust underpinned enduring pre-colonial Indian Ocean trade. (Correct):** Every sentence builds toward how seasonal monsoon winds and shared commercial trust—not naval conquest or imperial force—sustained vibrant Indian Ocean trade before European arrival.
- **A — European caravels introduced the first reliable long-distance navigation across the Indian Ocean.:** The passage states cosmopolitan port cities flourished "long before European caravels arrived".
- **C — Imperial naval fleets enforced trade monopolies between East Africa and the Malabar Coast.:** This directly contradicts the opening and closing sentences, which stress trade relied less on conquest and imperial decrees.
- **D — Ancient mariners avoided port cities because seasonal storms made coastal trade hazardous.:** Mariners timed voyages to predictable monsoons specifically to connect and sustain thriving port cities, not avoid them.
- **Exam Extra:** A strong central idea unifies all sentences in a paragraph rather than zooming in on one chronological comparison or minor detail.

### Q715 · Reading Comprehension - Inference

Transparency in medical artificial intelligence involves more than publishing complex source code that few clinicians can parse. An ethical diagnostic tool must explain, in plain clinical terms, which patient indicators drove its risk score. Without interpretable reasoning, physicians cannot responsibly challenge an erroneous recommendation when a life hangs in the balance.

According to the passage, why is source-code disclosure alone insufficient for ethical medical AI?

A. Source code is proprietary and routinely protected by international patent law.
B. Diagnostic models change too slowly to require clinical oversight during emergencies.
C. Clinicians need understandable explanations of specific predictions to exercise sound judgment.
D. Publishing source code exposes hospital patient records to external cyber threats.

**Answer:** C. Clinicians need understandable explanations of specific predictions to exercise sound judgment.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Clinicians need understandable explanations of specific predictions to exercise sound judgment. (Correct):** Because few clinicians can parse raw source code, ethical medical AI must explain in plain clinical terms which patient indicators shaped a prediction so doctors can question errors responsibly.
- **A — Source code is proprietary and routinely protected by international patent law.:** Patent law and proprietary restrictions are never discussed as barriers in the passage.
- **B — Diagnostic models change too slowly to require clinical oversight during emergencies.:** The passage stresses the need for timely clinical oversight precisely when patient lives are at stake.
- **D — Publishing source code exposes hospital patient records to external cyber threats.:** Cybersecurity and patient data privacy risks from code release are not mentioned in the text.
- **Exam Extra:** Watch for "more than X; it requires Y" structures in ethics passages—the core requirement always sits in the Y clause.

### Q716 · Reading Comprehension - Vocabulary in Context

Glacier retreat in high mountain ranges initially swells downstream rivers, creating an illusion of abundant freshwater. Yet once ice reserves shrink past a critical threshold, seasonal meltwater dwindles, leaving agrarian valleys vulnerable to acute drought. Policymakers who mistake this temporary surge for permanent supply invite *catastrophic* water scarcity.

Which word best captures the meaning of "catastrophic" as used in the passage?

A. Cyclical
B. Manageable
C. Imperceptible
D. Disastrous

**Answer:** D. Disastrous
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Disastrous (Correct):** Describing severe drought after ice reserves collapse past a critical threshold indicates that "catastrophic" means disastrous or ruinous.
- **A — Cyclical:** Cyclical suggests a repeating natural rhythm, whereas the passage warns of permanent loss once ice reserves shrink past a critical threshold.
- **B — Manageable:** Manageable understates the crisis, clashing with "acute drought" and "vulnerable".
- **C — Imperceptible:** Imperceptible means too slight to notice, whereas acute water scarcity has severe visible consequences.
- **Exam Extra:** In vocabulary-in-context items, check the gravity of the surrounding scenario ("acute drought", "mistake... permanent supply") to match intensity accurately.

### Q717 · Reading Comprehension - Cause and Effect

Digital platforms frequently promote "frictionless" design, trimming every confirmation step between a user's impulse and a purchase. While seamless checkout boosts short-term conversion rates, eliminating deliberate pauses can erode consumer reflection and fuel buyer's remorse. Introducing modest friction, such as a clear summary screen, protects trust without crippling usability.

Why does the author advocate preserving modest friction in digital checkout design?

A. Deliberate pauses encourage thoughtful decisions and protect long-term user trust.
B. Extra confirmation screens significantly increase platform advertising revenue.
C. Frictionless interfaces violate international banking encryption protocols.
D. Short-term conversion rates always rise when checkout takes more steps.

**Answer:** A. Deliberate pauses encourage thoughtful decisions and protect long-term user trust.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Deliberate pauses encourage thoughtful decisions and protect long-term user trust. (Correct):** The passage states that eliminating deliberate pauses fuels buyer's remorse, whereas a modest confirmation screen encourages reflection and protects long-term trust without harming usability.
- **B — Extra confirmation screens significantly increase platform advertising revenue.:** Advertising revenue is never linked to confirmation screens in the passage.
- **C — Frictionless interfaces violate international banking encryption protocols.:** Nothing suggests frictionless design breaks banking encryption rules; the critique focuses on user reflection and trust.
- **D — Short-term conversion rates always rise when checkout takes more steps.:** The passage notes that seamless (low-friction) design boosts short-term conversion, not that adding steps raises it.
- **Exam Extra:** When evaluating "why" questions on persuasive texts, connect the proposed solution directly to the negative consequence mentioned in the preceding sentence.

### Q718 · Reading Comprehension - Main Idea

Gross Domestic Product measures the market value of goods and services traded, yet it remains blind to unpaid caregiving and environmental degradation. A forest cleared for timber registers as economic growth, even if the loss of watershed protection triggers costly floods downstream. Therefore, supplementing GDP with natural-capital accounting offers a truer gauge of national prosperity.

What is the primary purpose of the passage?

A. To propose abolishing timber exports in developing economies
B. To argue for complementing GDP with metrics that reflect ecological and non-market realities
C. To prove that unpaid caregiving contributes more to flood prevention than forestry
D. To demonstrate why market-value accounting is superior to environmental regulation

**Answer:** B. To argue for complementing GDP with metrics that reflect ecological and non-market realities
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — To argue for complementing GDP with metrics that reflect ecological and non-market realities (Correct):** By showing that GDP ignores unpaid caregiving and environmental loss, the passage builds to its concluding recommendation: pairing GDP with natural-capital accounting for a truer measure of prosperity.
- **A — To propose abolishing timber exports in developing economies:** The timber example illustrates a flaw in how GDP counts growth, not a call to ban timber exports.
- **C — To prove that unpaid caregiving contributes more to flood prevention than forestry:** Caregiving and flood prevention are cited as two separate omissions from GDP, not compared against each other.
- **D — To demonstrate why market-value accounting is superior to environmental regulation:** The passage criticizes relying solely on market-value accounting when ecological costs are ignored.
- **Exam Extra:** Sentence-final words like "Therefore" often signal the author's main thesis or policy conclusion in short analytical passages.

### Q719 · Reading Comprehension - Tone and Attitude

Popular textbooks often portray the Industrial Revolution as a sudden triumph of steam engines and visionary inventors. Yet this tidy narrative overlooks the rural enclosures, child labour, and artisanal resistance that accompanied early factories. Revisiting those neglected voices does not belittle technological ingenuity; rather, it restores human complexity to a pivotal era.

How does the author view conventional textbook accounts of the Industrial Revolution?

A. As malicious fabrications intended to glorify modern factory owners
B. As exhaustive records that require no further historical investigation
C. As overly simplified narratives that omit significant social costs
D. As inaccurate because steam engines played almost no role in industrialization

**Answer:** C. As overly simplified narratives that omit significant social costs
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — As overly simplified narratives that omit significant social costs (Correct):** Calling textbook accounts a "tidy narrative" that "overlooks" enclosures, child labour, and resistance shows the author views them as oversimplified rather than deliberately dishonest or completely false.
- **A — As malicious fabrications intended to glorify modern factory owners:** Nothing suggests textbooks were written as malicious propaganda to glorify factory owners.
- **B — As exhaustive records that require no further historical investigation:** The author explicitly critiques these accounts for leaving out major social realities.
- **D — As inaccurate because steam engines played almost no role in industrialization:** The passage affirms technological ingenuity and never denies the importance of steam power.
- **Exam Extra:** Notice balanced qualifiers like "does not belittle technological ingenuity"—they rule out extreme negative answer choices.

### Q720 · Reading Comprehension - Inference

Whistleblowers who expose corporate fraud often face years of legal retaliation and career isolation, even when their disclosures save taxpayers millions. Offering anonymous reporting hotlines without ironclad legal protection is merely cosmetic accountability. Genuine institutional integrity demands shielding truth-tellers from reprisal before demanding their courage.

What can be inferred about the author's stance on corporate accountability?

A. Anonymous hotlines are sufficient to deter large-scale financial fraud.
B. Whistleblowers should bear personal legal costs to prove their motives are pure.
C. Financial savings rarely justify publicizing internal corporate misconduct.
D. Structural protections against retaliation are essential for ethical reporting systems to work.

**Answer:** D. Structural protections against retaliation are essential for ethical reporting systems to work.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Structural protections against retaliation are essential for ethical reporting systems to work. (Correct):** Dismissing hotlines without "ironclad legal protection" as "cosmetic" and urging shields against reprisal directly implies that anti-retaliation safeguards are vital for ethical reporting to function.
- **A — Anonymous hotlines are sufficient to deter large-scale financial fraud.:** The passage explicitly criticizes anonymous hotlines without strong legal shields as ineffective.
- **B — Whistleblowers should bear personal legal costs to prove their motives are pure.:** The author argues whistleblowers should be protected from retaliation, not saddled with legal burdens.
- **C — Financial savings rarely justify publicizing internal corporate misconduct.:** Disclosures are credited with saving taxpayers millions, directly affirming their public value.
- **Exam Extra:** Metaphors like "cosmetic accountability" mean surface-level fixes that fail without deeper structural backing.

### Q721 · Reading Comprehension - Factual Synthesis

Urban tree-planting campaigns often celebrate seedling counts while ignoring post-planting survival. Young saplings stranded in compacted roadside soil without moisture barriers frequently perish within two dry seasons. Shifting subsidies from sapling numbers to three-year survival benchmarks would transform ceremonial photo opportunities into lasting urban canopy.

What policy shift does the author recommend?

A. Tying urban forestry funding to multi-year tree survival rather than initial planting volume
B. Replacing roadside saplings with artificial shade structures in dry cities
C. Banning volunteer participation in municipal tree-planting drives
D. Restricting urban forestry budgets solely to parks with existing mature trees

**Answer:** A. Tying urban forestry funding to multi-year tree survival rather than initial planting volume
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Tying urban forestry funding to multi-year tree survival rather than initial planting volume (Correct):** The concluding sentence explicitly proposes shifting subsidies away from initial sapling numbers toward three-year survival benchmarks to build lasting urban tree cover.
- **B — Replacing roadside saplings with artificial shade structures in dry cities:** Artificial shade structures are never proposed as an alternative to urban trees.
- **C — Banning volunteer participation in municipal tree-planting drives:** Volunteer drives are not blamed; the flaw lies in funding planting counts instead of multi-year survival care.
- **D — Restricting urban forestry budgets solely to parks with existing mature trees:** The passage focuses on improving survival of newly planted roadside saplings, not restricting funds to mature parks.
- **Exam Extra:** Policy-recommendation questions usually align closely with hypothetical or "would transform" statements near the end of the passage.

### Q722 · Reading Comprehension - Tone and Attitude

Every decade heralds a supposedly revolutionary classroom gadget promised to render teachers obsolete. From instructional radio to tablet computers, hardware alone has repeatedly fallen short whenever pedagogy was treated as an afterthought. Technology amplifies skilled instruction; it cannot manufacture curiosity out of silicon.

Which phrase best describes the author's attitude toward technological determinism in education?

A. Enthusiastically supportive
B. Skeptical yet grounded
C. Indifferent and fatalistic
D. Hostile to all classroom tools

**Answer:** B. Skeptical yet grounded
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Skeptical yet grounded (Correct):** The author doubts exaggerated claims that gadgets make teachers obsolete, yet acknowledges that technology "amplifies skilled instruction"—a balanced, skeptical-yet-grounded stance.
- **A — Enthusiastically supportive:** The author critiques repeated gadget hype from instructional radio to tablets rather than endorsing it enthusiastically.
- **C — Indifferent and fatalistic:** The tone is analytical and constructive about pedagogy, not indifferent or resigned to failure.
- **D — Hostile to all classroom tools:** Calling technology an amplifier of skilled instruction contradicts total hostility toward classroom tools.
- **Exam Extra:** Distinguish between "skeptical" (questioning hype while recognizing limits) and "hostile" (rejecting technology outright). SSC often tests this exact nuance.

### Q723 · Reading Comprehension - Vocabulary in Context

In many emerging cities, street vendors form the *backbone* of affordable daily commerce, supplying fresh produce and cooked meals within walking distance of low-income neighbourhoods. Treating informal markets purely as traffic nuisances overlooks their vital role in urban food security. Designing designated vending zones balances pedestrian flow with livelihoods.

What does the metaphor "backbone" signify in the passage?

A. An outdated obstacle to modernization
B. A rigid legal barrier to urban planning
C. An essential foundational support
D. A minor supplement to supermarket chains

**Answer:** C. An essential foundational support
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — An essential foundational support (Correct):** Describing vendors as the "backbone" of affordable daily commerce and calling their role "vital" in urban food security shows the metaphor denotes essential foundational support.
- **A — An outdated obstacle to modernization:** The passage defends street vendors as essential today rather than portraying them as obsolete obstacles.
- **B — A rigid legal barrier to urban planning:** Legal barriers are not implied; the text urges creating designated vending zones to integrate them.
- **D — A minor supplement to supermarket chains:** Supermarkets are not mentioned, and calling vendors the "backbone" highlights primary importance, not a minor role.
- **Exam Extra:** Figurative words like "backbone", "linchpin", or "bedrock" almost always point to structural necessity or foundational importance.

### Q724 · Reading Comprehension - Cause and Effect

During the nineteenth century, the spread of standardized railway timetables forced distant towns to abandon local solar time. Previously, noon differed by several minutes between neighbouring provinces, creating scheduling chaos and collision risks on single-track lines. Standard time zones emerged less from astronomical curiosity than from the practical urgency of industrial transit.

What prompted the adoption of standardized time zones according to the passage?

A. A desire among astronomers to synchronize global observatory clocks
B. Government bans on municipal sundials in rural provinces
C. Competition between rival shipping companies over ocean navigation
D. Operational safety and scheduling demands of expanding railway networks

**Answer:** D. Operational safety and scheduling demands of expanding railway networks
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Operational safety and scheduling demands of expanding railway networks (Correct):** Scheduling chaos and collision risks on single-track lines created the practical urgency that drove towns to replace local solar noon with standardized railway time zones.
- **A — A desire among astronomers to synchronize global observatory clocks:** The passage explicitly notes standard time grew "less from astronomical curiosity" than from transit needs.
- **B — Government bans on municipal sundials in rural provinces:** Government bans on sundials are neither mentioned nor implied.
- **C — Competition between rival shipping companies over ocean navigation:** Maritime shipping competition is absent from the text, which focuses on single-track railways across provinces.
- **Exam Extra:** Watch for "less from X than from Y" constructions—they explicitly rule out X as the primary cause and point straight to Y.

### Q725 · Reading Comprehension - Main Idea

Consent forms written in dense legal jargon fulfill a narrow procedural checkbox while leaving research participants confused about real risks. Ethical informed consent requires plain language, time for questions, and explicit freedom to withdraw without penalty. Respecting human dignity means ensuring comprehension, not merely collecting signatures.

What is the main takeaway of the passage?

A. Meaningful informed consent depends on genuine participant understanding rather than legalistic paperwork.
B. Written consent forms should be abolished in favour of verbal agreements in clinical trials.
C. Participants rarely read consent documents even when written in simple, everyday language.
D. Legal liability can be avoided only by lengthening research disclosure forms.

**Answer:** A. Meaningful informed consent depends on genuine participant understanding rather than legalistic paperwork.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Meaningful informed consent depends on genuine participant understanding rather than legalistic paperwork. (Correct):** The passage contrasts legalistic box-ticking with true comprehension, plain language, and freedom to withdraw, concluding that dignity requires understanding rather than mere signatures.
- **B — Written consent forms should be abolished in favour of verbal agreements in clinical trials.:** The passage calls for plain-language consent and time for questions, not eliminating written forms altogether.
- **C — Participants rarely read consent documents even when written in simple, everyday language.:** Confusion arises from dense legal jargon, not because participants refuse to read clear documents.
- **D — Legal liability can be avoided only by lengthening research disclosure forms.:** Increasing length with legal jargon undermines comprehension instead of fulfilling ethical standards.
- **Exam Extra:** When two sentences state a flaw and the final sentence generalizes the principle ("Respecting human dignity means..."), that closing statement usually captures the main takeaway.

### Q726 · Reading Comprehension - Cause and Effect

Excessive synthetic fertilizer runoff washes nitrogen into coastal estuaries, triggering explosive algal blooms. When these dense algae die and decompose, microbes consume dissolved oxygen, leaving aquatic "dead zones" unable to sustain fish or shellfish. Precision application matched to crop uptake curbs both farmer expenses and marine suffocation.

How does excess fertilizer runoff ultimately create marine dead zones?

A. Chemical nitrogen directly poisons fish before algae can grow in estuaries.
B. Decomposing algal blooms deplete dissolved oxygen required by marine life.
C. Precision application blocks sunlight from reaching coastal shellfish beds.
D. Algal blooms absorb heavy metals that raise ocean water temperatures.

**Answer:** B. Decomposing algal blooms deplete dissolved oxygen required by marine life.
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Decomposing algal blooms deplete dissolved oxygen required by marine life. (Correct):** Excess nitrogen fuels dense algal blooms whose subsequent death and microbial decomposition strip dissolved oxygen from the water, suffocating fish and shellfish.
- **A — Chemical nitrogen directly poisons fish before algae can grow in estuaries.:** Nitrogen triggers algal overgrowth first; oxygen depletion during decomposition—not direct nitrogen toxicity—creates the dead zones.
- **C — Precision application blocks sunlight from reaching coastal shellfish beds.:** Precision application is presented as the solution that curbs runoff, not a cause of sunlight blockage.
- **D — Algal blooms absorb heavy metals that raise ocean water temperatures.:** Neither heavy metals nor ocean warming is mentioned in the passage.
- **Exam Extra:** Scientific cause-and-effect passages often test intermediate steps—verify the exact link (algae -> decomposition -> oxygen loss) rather than jumping straight from cause to effect.

### Q727 · Reading Comprehension - Vocabulary in Context

While open-source software relies on volunteer maintainers around the globe, critical digital infrastructure often rests on code maintained by a single *overburdened* developer. When a flaw surfaces in such a foundational library, corporations that profited freely from it scramble for patches. Sustainable open ecosystems require shared financial stewardship, not passive consumption.

In this context, "overburdened" most closely means:

A. Handsomely compensated
B. Technically unqualified
C. Excessively strained with workload
D. Reluctant to share source code

**Answer:** C. Excessively strained with workload
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Excessively strained with workload (Correct):** Referring to a single unpaid volunteer carrying the weight of critical digital infrastructure used by corporations indicates "overburdened" means excessively strained with workload.
- **A — Handsomely compensated:** Volunteers are described as lacking shared corporate financial support, not as highly paid.
- **B — Technically unqualified:** Foundational infrastructure rests on their code, implying high technical skill rather than incompetence.
- **D — Reluctant to share source code:** The developer actively maintains open-source code rather than withholding it.
- **Exam Extra:** Break unfamiliar compound words into parts: "over-" (excessively) + "burdened" (weighed down with tasks/responsibility).

### Q728 · Cloze Test - Contextual Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Coral reefs support nearly a quarter of all marine species despite covering less than one percent of the ocean floor. Rising sea temperatures, however, cause corals to expel the microscopic algae that nourish them, a stress response known as bleaching. If ocean warming continues unabated, these fragile ecosystems risk ______(1)______ loss that no short-term intervention can undo.

A. temporary
B. trivial
C. negligible
D. irreversible

**Answer:** D. irreversible
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — irreversible (Correct):** The clause "that no short-term intervention can undo" defines permanent, unfixable damage, making "irreversible" the only exact fit.
- **A — temporary:** A temporary loss could be undone quickly, contradicting the clause stating no short-term intervention can reverse it.
- **B — trivial:** Calling an entire ecosystem's collapse trivial clashes with "fragile ecosystems" and "warming continues unabated".
- **C — negligible:** Negligible means too small to matter, whereas ecosystem collapse is severe and lasting.
- **Exam Extra:** In Cloze Tests, relative clauses right after the blank ("that no short-term intervention can undo") often act as direct definitions of the missing word.

### Q729 · Cloze Test - Connectors and Transitions

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Cloud computing allows startups to scale server capacity within minutes instead of buying costly hardware upfront. ______(1)______, poorly monitored cloud usage can silently inflate monthly operating expenses. Engineers therefore rely on automated budget alerts to balance agility with cost discipline.

A. Nevertheless
B. Consequently
C. Furthermore
D. Similarly

**Answer:** A. Nevertheless
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Nevertheless (Correct):** The first sentence highlights a major benefit (instant scaling without upfront cost), while the second introduces a hidden drawback (inflated monthly bills), requiring the contrastive connector "Nevertheless".
- **B — Consequently:** Choosing "Consequently" would imply inflated costs are a logical goal or direct result of avoiding upfront hardware rather than a contrasting pitfall.
- **C — Furthermore:** Using "Furthermore" adds a supporting advantage, which clashes with the negative warning about silent cost inflation.
- **D — Similarly:** "Similarly" draws a parallel, whereas the second sentence shifts from an advantage to a financial risk.
- **Exam Extra:** Always check the logical relationship between the sentence before and after a blank—addition, cause-effect, or contrast—before choosing a conjunctive adverb.

### Q730 · Cloze Test - Collocations and Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

During periods of high inflation, households instinctively cut back on discretionary spending such as dining out and travel. This pullback can ______(1)______ broader economic growth because consumer demand accounts for the largest share of output in many nations. Policymakers must therefore stabilize prices without triggering a steep downturn.

A. accelerate
B. dampen
C. fabricate
D. validate

**Answer:** B. dampen
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — dampen (Correct):** "Dampen" collocates naturally with "economic growth" to mean slow down or restrain, matching the impact of reduced household spending.
- **A — accelerate:** Cutting back on spending slows economic activity rather than accelerating growth.
- **C — fabricate:** To fabricate means to invent or falsify, which makes no logical sense with broader economic growth.
- **D — validate:** To validate means to confirm or approve, which fails both grammar and economic logic here.
- **Exam Extra:** SSC Cloze Tests frequently reward standard business and economic collocations such as "dampen growth", "curb inflation", and "boost demand".

### Q731 · Cloze Test - Grammar and Prepositions

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

The Rosetta Stone proved indispensable to nineteenth-century scholars deciphering Egyptian hieroglyphs. Because the slab bore the same royal decree inscribed ______(1)______ three distinct scripts, linguists could cross-reference familiar Greek words against unknown symbols. That breakthrough unlocked centuries of forgotten records.

A. beside
B. across
C. in
D. upon

**Answer:** C. in
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — in (Correct):** Texts, decrees, and messages are idiomatically inscribed or written "in" a particular script or language ("inscribed in three distinct scripts").
- **A — beside:** "Beside" denotes physical location next to something, not the writing system used for the text.
- **B — across:** "Across" indicates span or movement over a region, whereas scripts are the medium of expression.
- **D — upon:** "Upon" fits a physical surface like a stone slab ("inscribed upon the slab"), not the scripts themselves.
- **Exam Extra:** Distinguish surface from medium with "inscribe": things are inscribed "on/upon" a stone or tablet, but inscribed "in" a language, script, or alphabet.

### Q732 · Cloze Test - Contextual Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Journalists covering sensitive public-interest leaks must verify every document through independent sources before publication. Speed without corroboration risks spreading misinformation and eroding public ______(1)______ in independent reporting. Rigorous fact-checking remains the bedrock of credible journalism.

A. hostility
B. indifference
C. curiosity
D. confidence

**Answer:** D. confidence
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — confidence (Correct):** "Eroding public confidence in independent reporting" is an exact contextual collocation matching the harm caused by unverified speed.
- **A — hostility:** Fact-checking aims to prevent distrust, and "eroding hostility" would mean reducing anger rather than harming credibility.
- **B — indifference:** Eroding indifference would imply making the public care more, contradicting the risk of spreading misinformation.
- **C — curiosity:** Curiosity does not naturally pair with "eroding ... in reporting" to describe journalistic credibility.
- **Exam Extra:** Look closely at the preposition after the blank ("in independent reporting")—it helps verify which noun forms a natural idiom ("confidence in").

### Q733 · Cloze Test - Phrasal Verbs

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

After repeated data breaches exposed weak password habits across workplaces, cybersecurity teams urged staff to adopt two-factor authentication. Although some employees initially resisted the extra login step, clear training helped ______(1)______ lingering doubts. Within two months, unauthorized login attempts dropped sharply.

A. clear away
B. draw out
C. put off
D. turn down

**Answer:** A. clear away
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — clear away (Correct):** "Clear away" means to remove or dispel obstacles, fitting seamlessly with "lingering doubts" after clear training.
- **B — draw out:** "Draw out" means to prolong or make something last longer, which opposes resolving employees' doubts.
- **C — put off:** "Put off" means to postpone or discourage someone, rather than reassuring staff during onboarding.
- **D — turn down:** "Turn down" means to reject a request or lower volume, neither of which collocates with doubts.
- **Exam Extra:** Memorize high-frequency SSC phrasal verbs with "clear": "clear away" (remove doubts/clutter), "clear up" (solve a mystery/weather improves), and "clear out" (empty a space).

### Q734 · Cloze Test - Grammar and Tense

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Over the past decade, urban planners in several dry regions have redesigned parks to capture seasonal rainwater. Instead of relying on concrete drains that rush runoff out of the city, sunken gardens now filter storm flows into local aquifers. Since these bioswales ______(1)______ five years ago, neighbourhood groundwater levels have risen measurably.

A. have been installing
B. were installed
C. are installing
D. will be installed

**Answer:** B. were installed
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — were installed (Correct):** With a specific past-time marker ("five years ago") in a "since"-clause paired with a present-perfect main clause ("have risen"), standard grammar requires the simple past passive "were installed".
- **A — have been installing:** "Have been installing" is active continuous, which incorrectly implies the bioswales themselves installed something and clashes with "five years ago".
- **C — are installing:** "Are installing" uses present continuous active, failing both the past time marker and passive subject ("bioswales").
- **D — will be installed:** "Will be installed" indicates future time, directly contradicting "five years ago" and observed groundwater gains.
- **Exam Extra:** Classic SSC tense rule: Present Perfect + "since" + Simple Past (especially when "ago" appears in the "since"-clause). Also check active vs. passive voice!

### Q735 · Cloze Test - Tone and Contrast

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

While headline unemployment figures capture national attention, they often mask regional disparities in job quality. A single-metric report may look ______(1)______ on paper even as rural wages stagnate and gig workers lack social security. Analysts therefore pair job counts with earnings and stability indicators.

A. dismal
B. chaotic
C. encouraging
D. redundant

**Answer:** C. encouraging
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — encouraging (Correct):** The phrase "even as rural wages stagnate" signals a contrast between upbeat headline figures and hidden economic distress, making "encouraging" the right fit.
- **A — dismal:** Calling the report "dismal" would align with stagnating wages rather than creating the contrast signaled by "even as".
- **B — chaotic:** "Chaotic" means disorderly or confused, which does not contrast with steady stagnation in wages and benefits.
- **D — redundant:** "Redundant" means unnecessarily repetitive, failing to describe how favourable a headline metric appears on paper.
- **Exam Extra:** Markers like "even as", "while", and "although" create structural tension; match the blank to the positive or negative pole opposite the contrasting clause.

### Q736 · Cloze Test - Contextual Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

For centuries, spices such as black pepper and cloves commanded astronomical prices in European markets because overland caravans faced steep tolls and arduous terrain. Seeking to bypass middlemen, maritime powers funded oceanic expeditions to reach Asian ports directly. Thus, culinary demand unexpectedly ______(1)______ global cartography and sea trade.

A. hindered
B. concealed
C. imitated
D. spurred

**Answer:** D. spurred
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — spurred (Correct):** "Spurred" means stimulated or propelled forward, accurately capturing how spice demand drove maritime exploration and mapmaking.
- **A — hindered:** "Hindered" means obstructed, contradicting the surge in oceanic expeditions and sea trade.
- **B — concealed:** "Concealed" means hid from view, whereas voyages expanded geographic knowledge.
- **C — imitated:** "Imitated" means copied, which does not make sense with "global cartography and sea trade".
- **Exam Extra:** Common SSC history-context verbs for growth and catalyst include "spurred", "fostered", "triggered", and "catalyzed".

### Q737 · Cloze Test - Connectors and Grammar

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Algorithmic credit-scoring tools promise faster loan approvals for applicants without traditional collateral. ______(1)______ their mathematical models are audited for hidden demographic biases, automated decisions risk locking vulnerable communities out of fair credit. Regulatory oversight must therefore keep pace with financial innovation.

A. Unless
B. Because
C. Whereas
D. Provided

**Answer:** A. Unless
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Unless (Correct):** "Unless" means "if not", expressing the negative condition under which unaudited models risk excluding vulnerable borrowers from fair credit.
- **B — Because:** "Because" turns auditing into the cause of exclusion, reversing the intended warning.
- **C — Whereas:** "Whereas" introduces a factual contrast between two clauses rather than a preventive condition.
- **D — Provided:** "Provided" means "on condition that", which would absurdly state that auditing models creates the risk of locking people out.
- **Exam Extra:** Test conditional conjunctions by mentally substituting "if ... not" for "unless" and "only if" for "provided"—the polarity check catches 90% of traps.

### Q738 · Cloze Test - Idioms and Collocations

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Wetlands act as natural sponges during heavy monsoons, absorbing floodwaters that would otherwise inundate nearby settlements. Yet rapid real-estate expansion continues to encroach upon marshlands in the ______(1)______ of urban development. Preserving these buffers is far cheaper than repairing flood-damaged infrastructure every few years.

A. absence
B. name
C. shadow
D. aftermath

**Answer:** B. name
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — name (Correct):** "In the name of" is the established English idiom meaning "using something as a justification or pretext for an action".
- **A — absence:** "In the absence of" means lacking development, contradicting the sentence's description of rapid real-estate expansion.
- **C — shadow:** "In the shadow of" suggests being overshadowed or neglected nearby, rather than justifying marshland encroachment.
- **D — aftermath:** "In the aftermath of" refers to the period following a disaster or event, not the motive driving expansion.
- **Exam Extra:** Prepositional idioms such as "in the name of", "in light of", and "at the expense of" are staples in SSC CHSL/CGL Cloze Tests.

### Q739 · Cloze Test - Contextual Adverbs

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Modern satellite imagery allows forest rangers to detect illegal logging camps almost as soon as clearings appear. Rather than waiting months for ground patrols to stumble upon remote sites, authorities can respond ______(1)______ to protect threatened habitats. Early intervention sharply curtails irreversible timber loss.

A. reluctantly
B. vaguely
C. promptly
D. scarcely

**Answer:** C. promptly
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — promptly (Correct):** Contrasted with "waiting months for ground patrols", "promptly" accurately conveys rapid, timely action enabled by real-time satellite imagery.
- **A — reluctantly:** "Reluctantly" implies hesitation, clashing with rangers' goal of protecting threatened habitats early.
- **B — vaguely:** "Vaguely" describes unclear expression, not the speed or decisiveness of an enforcement response.
- **D — scarcely:** "Scarcely" means barely or hardly, which contradicts curbing irreversible timber loss through early intervention.
- **Exam Extra:** Look for temporal contrast cues ("almost as soon as" vs. "waiting months") to identify adverbs of speed or timeliness.

### Q740 · Cloze Test - Grammar and Pronouns

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Public libraries serve as more than quiet book repositories in rapidly digitizing towns. For residents ______(1)______ lack high-speed home internet, library terminals provide essential access to job portals, tax forms, and online coursework. Sustaining these civic hubs bridges everyday opportunity gaps.

A. whom
B. whose
C. which
D. who

**Answer:** D. who
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — who (Correct):** "Who" is the subject relative pronoun required for people ("residents") when immediately followed by the finite verb "lack".
- **A — whom:** "Whom" is an objective-case pronoun and cannot serve as the subject of the verb "lack".
- **B — whose:** "Whose" shows possession and must be followed by a noun (e.g., "whose homes lack"), not directly by a verb.
- **C — which:** "Which" refers to objects or animals rather than human residents.
- **Exam Extra:** Quick relative-pronoun check: use "who" before a verb (subject), "whom" before a subject+verb (object), and "whose" before a noun (possession).

### Q741 · Cloze Test - Contextual Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

When a country relies heavily on a single export commodity, sharp swings in global prices can destabilize its entire national budget. Economists routinely advise resource-rich nations to ______(1)______ their industrial base by investing revenues into manufacturing and services. Broadening domestic production cushions the economy against sudden external shocks.

A. diversify
B. monopolize
C. shrink
D. isolate

**Answer:** A. diversify
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — diversify (Correct):** Moving from reliance on a single export to manufacturing and services while "broadening domestic production" precisely defines "diversify".
- **B — monopolize:** To monopolize means to concentrate control in one entity, worsening single-sector dependence rather than broadening it.
- **C — shrink:** To shrink the industrial base would heighten economic vulnerability rather than cushioning external shocks.
- **D — isolate:** To isolate the economy cuts off trade links instead of building resilient manufacturing and services.
- **Exam Extra:** In economy passages, antonym pairs like "single" vs. "broadening" almost always point to words such as "diversify" or "expand".

### Q742 · Cloze Test - Fixed Prepositions

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

True academic mentorship extends well beyond grading term papers or correcting lab techniques. Dedicated mentors take genuine pride ______(1)______ guiding students through intellectual setbacks as well as breakthroughs. That steady encouragement often determines whether first-generation scholars persist in research.

A. for
B. in
C. at
D. over

**Answer:** B. in
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — in (Correct):** The fixed noun-preposition phrase in English is "take pride in" (followed by a noun or gerund like "guiding").
- **A — for:** "For" does not pair idiomatically with "take pride" to introduce the source of satisfaction.
- **C — at:** "At" is used with emotions like "angry at" or "good at", not with the noun phrase "take pride".
- **D — over:** "Over" can pair with verbs like "glory over" or "quarrel over", but standard usage requires "in" after "take pride".
- **Exam Extra:** Keep the three "pride" prepositions distinct for SSC exams: "take pride in", "be proud of", and "pride oneself on".

### Q743 · Cloze Test - Phrasal Verbs

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Rural clinics often struggle to retain specialists when laboratory facilities and housing lag behind urban hospitals. Rather than penalizing doctors who seek transfers, health ministries can ______(1)______ chronic staffing shortages by upgrading clinic equipment and offering career-progression incentives. Addressing root working conditions yields lasting staffing stability.

A. bring about
B. pass over
C. stave off
D. fall back on

**Answer:** C. stave off
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — stave off (Correct):** "Stave off" means to ward off or prevent something undesirable from happening, fitting the goal of averting chronic staffing shortages.
- **A — bring about:** "Bring about" means to cause something to happen, which would mean creating shortages instead of preventing them.
- **B — pass over:** "Pass over" means to ignore or bypass someone for promotion, making no sense with staffing shortages.
- **D — fall back on:** "Fall back on" means to rely on a reserve option during difficulty, rather than actively preventing shortages.
- **Exam Extra:** "Stave off" (avert/delay) and "ward off" (repel/prevent) are favourite SSC CGL vocabulary phrasal verbs for mitigating crises.

### Q744 · Cloze Test - Contextual Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Single-use plastic wrappers persist in landfills and waterways for centuries after a five-minute meal. Packaging designers are therefore testing plant-based films that remain sturdy on store shelves yet break down rapidly in home compost bins. Replacing persistent polymers with ______(1)______ alternatives eases pressure on municipal waste systems.

A. hazardous
B. brittle
C. synthetic
D. biodegradable

**Answer:** D. biodegradable
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — biodegradable (Correct):** Films that "break down rapidly in home compost bins" are specifically "biodegradable", contrasting with "persistent polymers".
- **A — hazardous:** "Hazardous" means dangerous or toxic, which contradicts easing pressure on waste systems.
- **B — brittle:** "Brittle" means easily broken or fragile during handling, clashing with "remain sturdy on store shelves".
- **C — synthetic:** "Synthetic" describes human-made plastics, whereas the new films are plant-based and compostable.
- **Exam Extra:** Look for parallel contrast pairs in Cloze items: "persistent polymers" here directly cues its antonym, "biodegradable alternatives".

### Q745 · Cloze Test - Connectors and Transitions

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

In the early twentieth century, architectural conservationists began preserving ordinary worker cottages alongside grand palaces. They recognized that everyday dwellings reveal how most citizens actually cooked, worked, and gathered across generations. ______(1)______, historic preservation evolved from celebrating elite monuments to documenting shared social history.

A. Consequently
B. Conversely
C. Regardless
D. Otherwise

**Answer:** A. Consequently
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Consequently (Correct):** Recognizing the historical value of ordinary dwellings directly led to the broader evolution of historic preservation, making "Consequently" the exact cause-effect link.
- **B — Conversely:** "Conversely" signals an opposite trend, whereas expanding preservation to social history follows directly from valuing worker cottages.
- **C — Regardless:** "Regardless" means "despite that", implying preservation changed despite—not because of—new awareness.
- **D — Otherwise:** "Otherwise" introduces a hypothetical negative consequence if a condition is not met, which does not fit historical narrative.
- **Exam Extra:** Verify transition adverbs by checking if the final sentence states an outcome ("Consequently") or an unexpected reversal ("Conversely").

### Q746 · Cloze Test - Grammar and Subject-Verb Agreement

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Open-access scientific journals allow researchers in underfunded universities to read cutting-edge studies without paying steep subscription fees. Neither limited institutional budgets nor geographical distance ______(1)______ curious scholars from engaging with current discoveries anymore. Knowledge shared openly accelerates problem-solving across borders.

A. prevent
B. prevents
C. are preventing
D. have prevented

**Answer:** B. prevents
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — prevents (Correct):** With "Neither ... nor", the verb agrees with the nearer subject ("geographical distance"), which is singular and takes "prevents" in a general-truth statement.
- **A — prevent:** "Prevent" is plural, whereas the closer subject after "nor" ("geographical distance") is singular.
- **C — are preventing:** "Are preventing" is both plural and continuous, clashing with the singular nearer subject and general-truth adverb "anymore".
- **D — have prevented:** "Have prevented" uses the plural auxiliary "have", failing agreement with "geographical distance".
- **Exam Extra:** SSC Subject-Verb Agreement rule: For "Neither A nor B", "Either A or B", and "Not only A but also B", match the verb strictly to B (the closer noun).

### Q747 · Cloze Test - Collocations and Vocabulary

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Small-scale artisans often craft goods of remarkable quality yet struggle to reach buyers beyond local fairs. Digital cooperatives help bridge this gap by pooling logistics, photography, and payment processing under one shared storefront. By lowering distribution costs, collective platforms widen market ______(1)______ for rural creators.

A. congestion
B. restriction
C. access
D. surplus

**Answer:** C. access
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — access (Correct):** "Market access" is the standard economic phrase for the ability of producers to reach buyers across wider geographies.
- **A — congestion:** "Congestion" means overcrowding or blockage, which lowering distribution costs aims to reduce rather than widen.
- **B — restriction:** "Restriction" means limitation, directly contradicting the goal of helping artisans reach buyers beyond local fairs.
- **D — surplus:** "Surplus" refers to excess supply or budget, which does not pair naturally with "widen market ___ for creators".
- **Exam Extra:** Pairings like "market access", "market share", and "market volatility" are high-yield Cloze collocations—let the surrounding verb ("widen") guide your choice.

### Q748 · Cloze Test - Phrasal Verbs

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Public apologies from corporations after environmental spills often arrive wrapped in vague phrases that dodge direct responsibility. Citizens can easily ______(1)______ hollow statements when cleanup timelines remain missing or underfunded. Genuine accountability shows up in measurable restoration, not polished press releases.

A. fall for
B. abide by
C. count on
D. see through

**Answer:** D. see through
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — see through (Correct):** "See through" means to recognize the insincerity or hidden truth behind something deceptive, fitting "hollow statements".
- **A — fall for:** "Fall for" means to be deceived by something, which clashes with the critical tone noting citizens easily spot missing timelines.
- **B — abide by:** "Abide by" means to obey a rule or agreement, not to evaluate insincere corporate statements.
- **C — count on:** "Count on" means to rely upon or trust, the exact opposite of doubting hollow press releases.
- **Exam Extra:** Contrast "see through" (detect insincerity) with "fall for" (be fooled by); both appear often in SSC Tier-II comprehension and cloze sets.

### Q749 · Cloze Test - Contextual Adverbs

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Long-term clinical studies require patience because subtle lifestyle effects rarely appear in a matter of weeks. Researchers must track participants ______(1)______ across years to separate genuine health trends from random fluctuations. Premature conclusions drawn from brief trials often collapse upon replication.

A. consistently
B. impulsively
C. casually
D. erratically

**Answer:** A. consistently
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — consistently (Correct):** Distinguishing real long-term health trends from random fluctuations requires tracking participants "consistently" (steadily and uniformly) over years.
- **B — impulsively:** "Impulsively" means acting suddenly without foresight, undermining rigorous longitudinal research.
- **C — casually:** "Casually" suggests informal or careless observation, which introduces error rather than isolating subtle trends.
- **D — erratically:** "Erratically" means irregularly or unpredictably, which mimics random fluctuations instead of filtering them out.
- **Exam Extra:** Look for opposing concepts in the same sentence: "random fluctuations" signals the need for regularity and methodological steadiness ("consistently").

### Q750 · Cloze Test - Fixed Prepositions

Select the most appropriate option to fill in the blank:

Migratory birds navigate thousands of kilometres every autumn using starlight, magnetic fields, and coastal landmarks. However, blinding city skyglow can throw nocturnal flocks off course and leave them exhausted. Turning off decorative floodlights during peak migration weeks is fully compatible ______(1)______ urban safety.

A. to
B. with
C. for
D. against

**Answer:** B. with
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — with (Correct):** The adjective "compatible" standardly collocates with the preposition "with" ("compatible with urban safety").
- **A — to:** "To" follows adjectives such as "essential to" or "indifferent to", not "compatible".
- **C — for:** "For" indicates intended purpose or recipient rather than mutual harmony after "compatible".
- **D — against:** "Against" expresses opposition, which reverses the claim that dimming decorative floodlights can coexist safely with urban security.
- **Exam Extra:** Master fixed adjective-preposition pairs for SSC exams: "compatible with", "consistent with", "conducive to", and "detrimental to".
